\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)}\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}\Gamma(j)\Gamma(l)}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,x_{1}^{j-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,x_{2}^{l-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\times\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\Gamma(j+l+2z)\Gamma(1-j-l-\varepsilon-z)\Gamma(-z)\left(\frac{x_{1}x_{2}v_{12}}{2}\right)^{z}\,.

(113)

Now the Feynman parameter integrals are factorized and
can be evaluated in terms of beta function as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,x_{1}^{j+z-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,x_{2}^{l+z-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})=B(j+z,l+z)\,.

(114)

Using the Mellin-Barnes representation
for the Gauss hypergeometric function (
3.2.3
)
and Eq. (
114
) we get

\displaystyle I^{(0)}_{j,l}(v_{12};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)}\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}\Gamma(j)\Gamma(l)}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\Gamma(j+z)\Gamma(l+z)\Gamma(1-j-l-\varepsilon-z)\Gamma(-z)\left(\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)^{z}

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\,\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}}\frac{\Gamma(1-j-\varepsilon)\Gamma(1-l-\varepsilon)}{\Gamma^{2}(1-\varepsilon)}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(j,l,1-\varepsilon,1-\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\,\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}(1-j-\varepsilon)_{j}(1-l-\varepsilon)_{l}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(j,l,1-\varepsilon,1-\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)\,,

(115)

or alternatively by factorizing out the integral
I^{(0)}_{j+l}(\varepsilon)
:

\displaystyle I^{(0)}_{j,l}(v_{12};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}_{j+l}(\varepsilon)\,\frac{(1-j-l-\varepsilon)_{j}}{(1-j-\varepsilon)_{j}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(j,l,1-\varepsilon,1-\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)\,.

(116)

This result also holds for the previously excluded cases
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
.
As for the integral with one massive denominator we obtain a hypergeometric function
to be expanded in
\varepsilon
about integer parameters.

Using the Gauss theorem (
109
)
we obtain in the limit
v_{12}\rightarrow 0

\displaystyle I^{(0)}_{j,l}(0;\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}_{j+l}(\varepsilon)\,\frac{(1-j-l-\varepsilon)_{j}}{(1-j-\varepsilon)_{j}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(j,l,1-\varepsilon,1\right)

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}_{j+l}(\varepsilon)\,\frac{(1-j-l-\varepsilon)_{j}}{(1-j-\varepsilon)_{j}}\frac{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)\Gamma(1-j-l-\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(1-j-\varepsilon)\Gamma(1-l-\varepsilon)}=I^{(0)}_{j+l}(\varepsilon)\,.

(117)

One can see that the expressions (
3.2.4
)
and (
116
)
are manifestly symmetric under interchange
j\leftrightarrow l
, hence

I^{(0)}_{j,l}(v_{12};\varepsilon)=I^{(0)}_{l,j}(v_{12};\varepsilon)
.
From Eq.(
116
) it is evident that

I^{(0)}_{j,0}(v_{12};\varepsilon)=I^{(0)}_{j}(\varepsilon)
.

3.2.5
Single massive integral with two denominators

Now we consider the single massive case

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{1}{(v_{1}\cdot k)^{j}(v_{2}\cdot k)^{l}}\,,\qquad v_{11}\neq 0,\,\,v_{22}=0\,.

(118)

We can start our consideration after Feynman parametrization of the two denominator integral.
The vector
v
has now norm
v^{2}=x_{1}^{2}v_{11}+2x_{1}x_{2}v_{12}
. Employing again the Mellin-Barnes
representation of
I^{(1)}_{j+l}(v^{2})
gives
[compare with Eq. (
113
)]

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)}\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}\Gamma(j)\Gamma(l)}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,x_{1}^{j-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,x_{2}^{l-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\times\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\Gamma(j+l+2z)\Gamma(1-j-l-\varepsilon-z)\Gamma(-z)\left(\frac{x_{1}^{2}v_{11}}{4}+\frac{x_{1}x_{2}v_{12}}{2}\right)^{z}\,.

(119)

In order to evaluate the Feynman parameter integral following our strategy in
Sec.
3.2.4
, a factorization of

\left(\frac{x_{1}^{2}v_{11}}{4}+\frac{x_{1}x_{2}v_{12}}{2}\right)^{z}
is required. Applying the
Binomi-Mellin-Newton integral
representation
2
2

2

This integral representation does not have
a name in the literature
(see, e.g., Eq. (5.1) in Ref.
Smirnov:2012gma
, where it is just
referred to as a simple formula, however due to its frequent occurrence
and usefulness, it deserves, at least in the authors’ opinion,
a name analogous to the well-established Cahen-Mellin integral.
As (
416
) is the Mellin integral
version of Newton’s Binomial Theorem this name suggests itself.

\displaystyle(a+b)^{z_{1}}=\frac{1}{\Gamma(-z_{1})}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}a^{z_{1}-z_{2}}b^{z_{2}}\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(-z_{1}+z_{2})\,,

(120)

on this term, results in

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)}\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}\Gamma(j)\Gamma(l)}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\Gamma(j+l+2z_{1})\Gamma(1-j-l-\varepsilon-z_{1})

\displaystyle\times\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\left(\frac{v_{11}}{4}\right)^{z_{1}-z_{2}}\left(\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)^{z_{2}}\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(-z_{1}+z_{2})

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,x_{1}^{j+2z_{1}-z_{2}-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,x_{2}^{l+z_{2}-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})\,.

(121)

Evaluating the Feynman parameter integral again cancels the
\Gamma(j+l+2z_{1})
and yields

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)}\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}\Gamma(j)\Gamma(l)}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\left(\frac{v_{11}}{4}\right)^{z_{1}}\left(\frac{2v_{12}}{v_{11}}\right)^{z_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma(1-j-l-\varepsilon-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{1}+z_{2})\Gamma(j+2z_{1}-z_{2})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(l+z_{2})\,.

(122)

Performing the substitutions
z_{1}\rightarrow 1-j-l-\varepsilon+z_{1}
and
z_{2}\rightarrow 1-j-l-\varepsilon+z_{1}-z_{2}

results in the two-fold Mellin-Barnes representation

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)}\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}\Gamma(j)\Gamma(l)}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\left(\frac{v_{11}}{4}\right)^{1-j-l-\varepsilon+z_{1}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{2v_{12}}{v_{11}}\right)^{1-j-l-\varepsilon+z_{1}-z_{2}}\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(1-l-\varepsilon+z_{1}+z_{2})

\displaystyle\times\Gamma(-1+j+l+\varepsilon-z_{1}+z_{2})\Gamma(1-j-\varepsilon+z_{1}-z_{2})\,.

(123)

Replacing the product of the last two gamma functions in
Eq. (
3.2.5
) by the corresponding beta function
integral representation

\displaystyle\Gamma(-1+j+l+\varepsilon-z_{1}+z_{2})\Gamma(1-j-\varepsilon+z_{1}-z_{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma(l)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{-2+j+l+\varepsilon-z_{1}+z_{2}}(1-t)^{-j-\varepsilon+z_{1}-z_{2}}\,,

(124)

leads to

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)}\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{2^{j+l}\Gamma(j)}\left(\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)^{1-j-l-\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{-2+j+l+\varepsilon}(1-t)^{-j-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\left(\frac{1-t}{t}\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)^{z_{1}}\left(\frac{t}{1-t}\frac{v_{11}}{2v_{12}}\right)^{z_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(1-l-\varepsilon+z_{1}+z_{2})\,.

(125)

The appearing two-fold Mellin-Barnes integral
evaluates by the Binomi-Mellin-Newton integral to

\displaystyle\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\left(\frac{1-t}{t}\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)^{z_{1}}\left(\frac{t}{1-t}\frac{v_{11}}{2v_{12}}\right)^{z_{2}}\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(1-l-\varepsilon+z_{1}+z_{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma(1-l-\varepsilon)\left(1+\frac{1-t}{t}\frac{v_{12}}{2}+\frac{t}{1-t}\frac{v_{11}}{2v_{12}}\right)^{l-1+\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Gamma(1-l-\varepsilon)\left(\frac{v_{12}}{2t(1-t)}\right)^{l-1+\varepsilon}\left[1-t\left(2-\frac{2}{v_{12}}\right)+t^{2}\left(1-\frac{2}{v_{12}}+\frac{v_{11}}{v_{12}^{2}}\right)\right]^{l-1+\varepsilon}\,.

\displaystyle=\Gamma(1-l-\varepsilon)\left(\frac{v_{12}}{2t(1-t)}\right)^{l-1+\varepsilon}(1-\tau_{+}t)^{l-1+\varepsilon}(1-\tau_{-}t)^{l-1+\varepsilon}\,,

(126)

where we introduced the notation

\tau_{\pm}=1-(1\pm\sqrt{1-v_{11}})/v_{12}
.
Plugging this into integral (
125
) gives

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{2^{l}v_{12}^{j}}\,\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{(1-l-\varepsilon)_{l}\Gamma(j)}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{j-1}(1-t)^{1-j-l-2\varepsilon}(1-\tau_{+}t)^{l-1+\varepsilon}(1-\tau_{-}t)^{l-1+\varepsilon}\,.

(127)

The
t
-integral is the Euler integral representation of the
Appell function
F_{1}(a,b,c,d,x,y)
:

\displaystyle F_{1}(a,b,c,d,x,y)=\frac{\Gamma(c)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(d-a)}\,\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{a-1}(1-t)^{d-a-1}(1-xt)^{-b}(1-yt)^{-c}\,.

(128)

Here for completeness we also present the definition of the
Appell function
in terms of the Pochhammer symbol

\displaystyle F_{1}(a,b,c,d,x,y)=\sum\limits_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\,\frac{(a)_{m+n}(b)_{m}(c)_{n}}{(d)_{m+n}}\,\frac{x^{m}y^{n}}{m!n!}\,.

(129)

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{2^{l}v_{12}^{j}}\,\frac{(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)_{j+l}}{(1-l-\varepsilon)_{l}}\frac{\Gamma(2-j-l-2\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(2-l-2\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle\times F_{1}(j,1-l-\varepsilon,1-l-\varepsilon,2-l-2\varepsilon,\tau_{+},\tau_{-})\,.

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{2^{l}v_{12}^{j}}\,\frac{(2-l-2\varepsilon)_{l}}{(1-l-\varepsilon)_{l}}F_{1}(j,1-l-\varepsilon,1-l-\varepsilon,2-l-2\varepsilon,\tau_{+},\tau_{-})

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}_{l}(\varepsilon)}{v_{12}^{j}}F_{1}(j,1-l-\varepsilon,1-l-\varepsilon,2-l-2\varepsilon,\tau_{+},\tau_{-})\,.

(130)

As in Sec.
3.2.4
this result
is valid for all
j
and
l
even though
the derivation assumed that neither of them is a non-positive integer.
Employing the transformation

\displaystyle F_{1}(a,b_{1},b_{2},c,x,y)=(1-x)^{-b_{1}}(1-y)^{-b_{2}}F_{1}\left(c-a,b_{1},b_{2},c,\frac{x}{x-1},\frac{y}{y-1}\right)\,,

(131)

gives the alternative representation

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)=\frac{I^{(0)}_{l}(\varepsilon)}{v_{12}^{j}}\left(\frac{v_{11}}{v_{12}^{2}}\right)^{l-1+\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\times F_{1}\left(2-j-l-2\varepsilon,1-l-\varepsilon,1-l-\varepsilon,2-l-2\varepsilon,\frac{\tau_{+}}{\tau_{+}-1},\frac{\tau_{-}}{\tau_{-}-1}\right)\,,

(132)

which is a suitable starting point for the
\varepsilon
-expansion
in the case of positive integer
j
and
l
.

For
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
the hypergeometric series terminates at

m+n=-j
, since
(j)_{m+n}=0
otherwise.
Therefore,

\displaystyle F_{1}(j,1-l-\varepsilon,1-l-\varepsilon,2-l-2\varepsilon,x,y)=\sum_{m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(j)_{m+n}(1-l-\varepsilon)_{m}(1-l-\varepsilon)_{n}}{(2-l-2\varepsilon)_{m+n}}\,\frac{x^{m}}{m!}\frac{y^{n}}{n!}

\displaystyle\overset{j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}}{=}\sum_{m,n\leq-j}\frac{(j)_{m+n}(1-l-\varepsilon)_{m}(1-l-\varepsilon)_{n}}{(2-l-2\varepsilon)_{m+n}}\,\frac{x^{m}}{m!}\frac{y^{n}}{n!}

\displaystyle\,,

(133)

which makes the
\varepsilon
-expansion of
I^{(1)}_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)
trivial.

For
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
the Appell function
reduces to a sum of hypergeometric functions. It holds

\displaystyle F_{1}(j,1-l-\varepsilon,1-l-\varepsilon,2-l-2\varepsilon,x,y)

\displaystyle\overset{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}}{=}\frac{(2-2l-2\varepsilon)_{l}}{(1-y)^{j}}\sum_{n=0}^{-l}\binom{-l}{n}(-1)^{l-n}\frac{(j)_{n}\,\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}\left(j+n,1-l-\varepsilon,2-2l-2\varepsilon,\frac{x-y}{1-y}\right)}{(1-y)^{n}(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)_{l+n}}\,.

(134)

This form allows for a much simpler
\varepsilon-
expansion since we reduced the two-variable
Appell function
to a finite sum of genuinely one-variable functions.

3.2.6
Double massive integral with two denominators

An approach similar to the massless and single-massive case yields a complicated three-fold
Mellin-Barnes integral
(see Ref.
Somogyi:2011ir
). Even though this might be a good starting point for numerically
establishing
\varepsilon
-expansions, there has been
no successful attempt to obtain a closed analytic expression similar
to the single-massive case
Somogyi:2011ir
.
We will close this gap in Sec.
3.3.1

For the purpose of
\varepsilon
-expansion,
we propose the following promising new method. By restricting ourselves to
j,l\in\mathbb{N}
we obtain
a closed expression for the double massive integral for arbitrary
D
by employing
the generalized two-mass splitting
formula (
476
).
This reduces the double massive integral to a sum of
j+l
single-massive integrals.
For
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
we use Eq. (
494
)
for reduction to single massive integrals.

Using the generalized two-point splitting lemma
[see details in Appendix
D
]
we get

\displaystyle I_{j,l}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{1}{(v_{1}\cdot k)^{j}(v_{2}\cdot k)^{l}}=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\Delta^{j}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\left[\sum_{n=0}^{j-1}\binom{l-1+n}{l-1}\lambda_{\pm}^{l}(1-\lambda_{\pm})^{n}\Delta^{j-n}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l+n}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v^{\pm}_{3}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hphantom{\,\,\,\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}}+\sum_{n=0}^{l-1}\binom{j-1+n}{j-1}\lambda_{\pm}^{n}(1-\lambda_{\pm})^{j}\Delta^{l-n}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\Delta^{j+n}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v^{\pm}_{3}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{j-1}\binom{l-1+n}{l-1}\lambda_{\pm}^{l}(1-\lambda_{\pm})^{n}\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\Delta^{j-n}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l+n}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v^{\pm}_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{l-1}\binom{j-1+n}{j-1}\lambda_{\pm}^{n}(1-\lambda_{\pm})^{j}\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\Delta^{l-n}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\Delta^{j+n}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v^{\pm}_{3}}\right)\,,

(135)

where
\left(v_{3}^{\pm}\right)^{2}=0
, therefore the double massive integral
with
j,l\in\mathbb{N}
can be evaluated for general
\varepsilon
as

\displaystyle I_{j,l}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{j-1}\binom{l-1+n}{l-1}\lambda_{\pm}^{l}(1-\lambda_{\pm})^{n}I^{(1)}_{j-n,l+n}(v_{13}^{\pm},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{l-1}\binom{j-1+n}{j-1}\lambda_{\pm}^{n}(1-\lambda_{\pm})^{j}I^{(1)}_{l-n,j+n}(v_{23}^{\pm},v_{22};\varepsilon)\,.

(136)

where

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{12}-v_{11}\pm\sqrt{v_{12}^{2}-v_{11}v_{22}}}{2v_{12}-v_{11}-v_{22}}\,,

\displaystyle v_{13}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=(1-\lambda_{\pm})v_{11}+\lambda_{\pm}v_{12}=\frac{v_{11}\left(v_{22}\pm\sqrt{v_{12}^{2}-v_{11}v_{22}}\right)-v_{12}\left(v_{12}\pm\sqrt{v_{12}^{2}-v_{11}v_{22}}\right)}{v_{11}+v_{22}-2v_{12}}\,,

\displaystyle v_{23}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=(1-\lambda_{\pm})v_{12}+\lambda_{\pm}v_{22}=\frac{v_{22}\left(v_{11}\mp\sqrt{v_{12}^{2}-v_{11}v_{22}}\right)-v_{12}\left(v_{12}\mp\sqrt{v_{12}^{2}-v_{11}v_{22}}\right)}{v_{11}+v_{22}-2v_{12}}\,.

(137)

Formula (
136
) holds for both sign choices.

When the power of one of the massive propagators is negative one can derive a simpler expression for the corresponding angular integral.
In particular, using Eqs. (
D.4
) and (
D.4
), it holds

\displaystyle I_{j,l}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{-j}\binom{-j}{n}\left(1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}\,\right)^{-j-n}\left(1-v_{11}\right)^{n/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times I_{l,-n}^{(1)}\left(1-\frac{1-v_{12}}{\sqrt{1-v_{11}}},v_{22};\varepsilon\right)\,,

(138)

for
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
and equivalently

\displaystyle I_{j,l}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{-l}\binom{-l}{n}\left(1-\sqrt{1-v_{22}}\,\right)^{-l-n}\left(1-v_{22}\right)^{n/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times I_{j,-n}^{(1)}\left(1-\frac{1-v_{12}}{\sqrt{1-v_{22}}},v_{11};\varepsilon\right)\,,

(139)

for
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\leq 0}
.

3.3
New properties of angular integrals

In this section we review new properties of angular integrals proposed and
developed in present paper for the first time. In particular, we
derive: (1) hypergeometric representation of the general two denominator angular integral
in
D
dimensions; (2) differential, partial integration, and recursion relations
for angular integrals. The main advantage of our findings is that it allow
to reduce all known angular integrals to a small set of basis integrals.

3.3.1
Hypergeometric representation of angular integrals

It is known (see, e.g., detailed discussion in Ref.
Somogyi:2011ir
)
that many angular integrals are represented in terms of hypergeometric (Gauss, Appell) functions.
In this section we demonstrate how to derive the
hypergeometric representation of the general angular integral having two denominators
in terms of the Lauricella function
F_{B}^{(3)}
.
The latter being a three-variable generalization of the Gauss and Appell function.

For this purpose we consider generic angular integral in
D=4-2\varepsilon
dimensions

\displaystyle I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\,\frac{1}{(v_{1}\cdot k)^{j}(v_{2}\cdot k)^{l}}\,,

(140)

where as before

\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}=\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\sin^{1-2\varepsilon}\theta_{1}\,\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}\sin^{-2\varepsilon}\theta_{2}

and
v_{ij}=v_{i}\cdot v_{j}
.

Using Feynman parametrization one gets

\displaystyle I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)=B(j,l)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,x_{1}^{j-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,x_{2}^{l-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})\,I^{(1)}_{j+l}(w_{12};\varepsilon)\,,

(141)

where
w_{12}=(x_{1}v_{1}+x_{2}v_{2})^{2}
is the Feynman “mass parameter”.

The one denominator massive integral
I^{(1)}_{j+l}(v,\varepsilon)
is given by

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j+l}(v;\varepsilon)=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{j+l}{2},\frac{j+l+1}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon,1-v\right)\,.

(142)

Due to the
\delta
-function
it follows
2x_{1}x_{2}=1-x_{1}^{2}-x_{2}^{2}
, and therefore
w_{12}

can be presented in the form, where the variables
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
are separated,

\displaystyle w_{12}=x_{1}^{2}v_{11}+2x_{1}x_{2}v_{12}+x_{2}^{2}v_{22}=v_{12}-x_{1}^{2}(v_{12}-v_{11})-x_{2}^{2}(v_{12}-v_{22})\,.

(143)

Representation (
143
) is very useful since it leads to
a Mellin-Barnes integral, which is considerably simpler than that derived
in Ref.
Somogyi:2011ir
. In addition, it can be expressed in terms of
the known hypergeometric function
F_{B}^{(3)}
. Using Eq. (
143
),
the Mellin-Barnes representation of the Gauss hypergeometric function

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c,x)=\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\frac{\left(a\right)_{z}\left(b\right)_{z}}{\left(c\right)_{z}}\Gamma(-z)\left(-x\right)^{z}\,,

(144)

and the Newton-Binomi-Mellin integral

\displaystyle(a+b+c)^{z}=\frac{1}{\Gamma(-z)}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\,a^{z-z_{1}-z_{2}}\,b^{z_{1}}\,c^{z_{2}}\,\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(-z+z_{1}+z_{2})\,,

(145)

we obtain

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j+l}\left(w_{12};\varepsilon\right)=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\Gamma(-z)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(-(1-v_{12})-x_{1}^{2}(v_{12}-v_{11})-x_{2}^{2}(v_{12}-v_{22})\right)^{z}\,\frac{\left(\frac{j+l}{2}\right)_{z}\left(\frac{j+l+1}{2}\right)_{z}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\right)_{z}}

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(-z+z_{1}+z_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\times(v_{11}-v_{12})^{z_{1}}(v_{22}-v_{12})^{z_{2}}(v_{12}-1)^{z-z_{1}-z_{2}}\frac{\left(\frac{j+l}{2}\right)_{z}\left(\frac{j+l+1}{2}\right)_{z}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\right)_{z}}\,x_{1}^{2z_{1}}x_{2}^{2z_{2}}

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{3}}{2\pi i}\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(-z_{3})

\displaystyle\qquad\times(v_{11}-v_{12})^{z_{1}}(v_{22}-v_{12})^{z_{2}}(v_{12}-1)^{z_{3}}\frac{\left(\frac{j+l}{2}\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}\left(\frac{j+l+1}{2}\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}}\,x_{1}^{2z_{1}}x_{2}^{2z_{2}}\,.

(146)

In the last step of derivation of Eq. (
3.3.1
) we changed variable
z\to z_{3}=z-z_{1}-z_{2}
.
The combination of the Pochhammer symbols
can be presented in the following form

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\frac{j+l}{2}\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}\left(\frac{j+l+1}{2}\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4^{-z_{1}-z_{2}}\left(j+l\right)_{2z_{1}+2z_{2}}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\left(\frac{j+l}{2}+z_{1}+z_{2}\right)_{z_{3}}\left(\frac{j+l+1}{2}+z_{1}+z_{2}\right)_{z_{3}}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon+z_{1}+z_{2}\right)_{z_{3}}}\,,

(147)

whereby the
z_{3}
integral becomes
a Gauss hypergeometric function,

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{j+l}\left(w_{12};\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\left(\frac{v_{11}-v_{12}}{4}\right)^{z_{1}}\left(\frac{v_{22}-v_{12}}{4}\right)^{z_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\frac{\left(j+l\right)_{2z_{1}+2z_{2}}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}}}\,x_{1}^{2z_{1}}x_{2}^{2z_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{j+l}{2}+z_{1}+z_{2},\frac{j+l+1}{2}+z_{1}+z_{2},\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon+z_{1}+z_{2},1-v_{12}\right)\,.

(148)

Next to reduce the number of occurrences of the integration variables
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
in the
arguments of the Gauss hypergeometric function
we perform the Euler transformation

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{j+l}{2}+z_{1}+z_{2},\frac{j+l+1}{2}+z_{1}+z_{2},\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon+z_{1}+z_{2},1-v_{12}\right)

\displaystyle=\,\,

\displaystyle v_{12}^{1-j-l-\varepsilon-z_{1}-z_{2}}\,\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{3-j-l-2\varepsilon}{2},\frac{2-j-l-2\varepsilon}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon+z_{1}+z_{2},1-v_{12}\right)\,,

(149)

Introducing again the Mellin-Barnes representation
of the
Gauss hypergeometric function (
144
)
and combining the Pochhammer symbols
leads to

\displaystyle\hskip-7.11317ptI^{(1)}_{j+l}\left((x_{1}v_{1}+x_{2}v_{2})^{2};\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)v_{12}^{1-j-l-\varepsilon}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{3}}{2\pi i}

(150)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(-z_{3})\left(\frac{v_{11}-v_{12}}{4v_{12}}\right)^{z_{1}}\left(\frac{v_{22}-v_{12}}{4v_{12}}\right)^{z_{2}}\left(\frac{v_{12}-1}{4}\right)^{z_{3}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle x_{1}^{2z_{1}}x_{2}^{2z_{2}}\frac{\left(j+l\right)_{2z_{1}+2z_{2}}\left(2-j-l-2\varepsilon\right)_{2z_{3}}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}}\,.

Plugging this into expression (
141
) for
I_{j,l}
and
evaluating the Feynman parameter integral as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{B(j,l)}\,\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\,x_{1}^{j-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}\,x_{2}^{l-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})x_{1}^{2z_{1}}x_{2}^{2z_{2}}=\frac{\left(j\right)_{2z_{1}}\left(l\right)_{2z_{2}}}{\left(j+l\right)_{2z_{1}+2z_{2}}}\,,

(151)

yields the three-fold Mellin-Barnes representation

\displaystyle I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\,v_{12}^{1-j-l-\varepsilon}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{3}}{2\pi i}\,\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(-z_{3})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\left(\frac{v_{11}-v_{12}}{4v_{12}}\right)^{z_{1}}\left(\frac{v_{22}-v_{12}}{4v_{12}}\right)^{z_{2}}\left(\frac{v_{12}-1}{4}\right)^{z_{3}}\frac{\left(j\right)_{2z_{1}}\left(l\right)_{2z_{2}}\left(2-j-l-2\varepsilon\right)_{2z_{3}}}{\left(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}}\,.

(152)

Upon employing the duplication identity

\left(2x\right)_{2n}=2^{2n}\left(x\right)_{n}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\right)_{n}

on each Pochhammer symbol
in the numerator we obtain the
hypergeometric representation
of
I_{j,l}
in term of the Lauricella function

F_{B}^{(3)}
:

\displaystyle I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)=\frac{2\pi}{1-2\varepsilon}\,v_{12}^{1-j-l-\varepsilon}\,F_{B}^{(3)}\left(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},c;x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\right)\,,

(153)

where

\displaystyle a_{1}=\frac{j}{2}\,,\quad a_{2}=\frac{l}{2}\,,\quad a_{3}=\frac{3}{2}-a_{1}-a_{2}-\varepsilon\,,\quad b_{1}=a_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\,,\quad b_{2}=a_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,,\quad b_{3}=a_{3}-\frac{1}{2}\,,

\displaystyle c=a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}\,,\quad x_{1}=1-\frac{v_{11}}{v_{12}}\,,\quad x_{2}=1-\frac{v_{22}}{v_{12}}\,,\quad x_{3}=1-v_{12}\,.

(154)

Here the Lauricella function
F_{B}^{(3)}

is equivalently defined as the Mellin-Barnes integral

\displaystyle F_{B}^{(3)}\left(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},c,x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{3}}{2\pi i}\,\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(-z_{3})

(155)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(-x_{1})^{z_{1}}\,(-x_{2})^{z_{2}}\,(-x_{3})^{z_{3}}\,

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{\left(a_{1}\right)_{z_{1}}\,\left(a_{2}\right)_{z_{2}}\,\left(a_{3}\right)_{z_{3}}\,\left(b_{1}\right)_{z_{1}}\,\left(b_{2}\right)_{z_{2}}\,\left(b_{3}\right)_{z_{3}}}{\left(c\right)_{z_{1}+z_{2}+z_{3}}}\,.

or as the sum in terms of the Pochhammer symbols

\displaystyle F_{B}^{(3)}\left(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},c,x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m,n,p=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(a_{1}\right)_{m}\left(a_{2}\right)_{n}\left(a_{3}\right)_{p}\left(b_{1}\right)_{m}\left(b_{2}\right)_{n}\left(b_{3}\right)_{p}}{\left(c\right)_{m+n+p}}\,

(156)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{x_{1}^{m}}{m!}\frac{x_{2}^{n}}{n!}\frac{x_{3}^{p}}{p!}\,.

Eqs. (
153
)-(
156
)
represent the main result of this section —

Hypergeometric representation

for generic angular integral with two denominators
without referring to their mass-shell properties (i.e. they are massless or massive).
In Appendix
E

we explicitly demonstrate the consistency with known special cases.

3.3.2
Partial differential identities

In this section we derive differential identities involving angular integrals,
which help to establish recurrence relations between the latter. Note,
there were before some efforts to obtain differential relations between

Neerven-type integrals in literature (see, e.g.,
Ref.
Bojak:2000eu
). Here we perform a study of differential properties
of angular integrals in a systematic way.
For that purpose we consider the generic angular integral with two denominators

\displaystyle I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{1}{(v_{1}\cdot k)^{j}(v_{2}\cdot k)^{l}}=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\Delta^{j}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\,.

(157)

It is convenient to choose coordinates such that

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\mathbf{v_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{k}}=\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}\cos\theta_{1}}\,,

(158)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\mathbf{v_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{k}}=\frac{1}{1-\beta_{2}\cos\vartheta\cos\theta_{1}-\beta_{2}\sin\vartheta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}}\,,

(159)

with

\displaystyle\beta_{1}=\sqrt{1-v_{11}}\,,\qquad\beta_{2}=\sqrt{1-v_{22}}\,,

\displaystyle\cos\vartheta=\frac{1-v_{12}}{\sqrt{1-v_{11}}\sqrt{1-v_{22}}}\,,

\displaystyle\Delta_{12}=(\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\sin\vartheta)^{2}=(1-v_{11})(1-v_{22})-(1-v_{12})^{2}\,.

(160)

Taking partial derivatives of the propagators gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta_{1}}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\cos\theta_{1}\Delta^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\,,

(161)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta_{2}}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=(\cos\vartheta\cos\theta_{1}+\sin\vartheta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2})\Delta^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\,,

(162)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\beta_{2}(-\sin\vartheta\cos\theta_{1}+\cos\vartheta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2})\Delta^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\,.

(163)

Expressing appearing angular prefactors through the propagators yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta_{i}}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta_{i}}\,L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{i}}\right)\,\Delta^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{i}}\right)\,,

(164)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\cot\vartheta\left[L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)-\frac{\beta_{2}}{\beta_{1}\cos\vartheta}\,L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\right]\,\Delta^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\,,

(165)

where
L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v}\right)=1-\Delta^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v}\right)
.
Using these differential identities we can calculate the derivatives of
I_{j,l}

with respect to
\beta_{1}
,
\beta_{2}
, and
\vartheta
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta_{1}}I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta_{1}}\Delta^{j}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{j}{\beta_{1}}\,\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\,L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\,\Delta^{j-1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\Delta^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{j}{\beta_{1}}\,\Big(I_{j+1,l}-I_{j,l}\Big)\,,

(166)

and by analogy

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta_{2}}I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\frac{l}{\beta_{2}}(I_{j,l+1}-I_{j,l})\,,

(167)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=l\cot\vartheta\left(\left(1-\frac{\beta_{2}}{\beta_{1}\cos\vartheta}\right)I_{j,l+1}-I_{j,l}+\frac{\beta_{2}}{\beta_{1}\cos\vartheta}I_{j-1,l+1}\right)\,.

(168)

Due to rotational invariance the angular integral
I_{j,l}
does not
change under replacement of orientation of vectors
v_{1}
and
v_{2}

with respect to integration momentum
k
. This helps to
get the identity equivalent to Eq. (
168
):

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=j\cot\vartheta\left(\left(1-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}\cos\vartheta}\right)I_{j+1,l}-I_{j,l}+\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{2}\cos\vartheta}I_{j+1,l-1}\right)\,.

(169)

Next, we derive the differential identities for angular integrals
with respect to the set of variables
(v_{11},v_{22},v_{12})

using relations (
3.3.2
) and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{12}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\sin\vartheta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\,,

(170)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{11}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\beta_{1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta_{1}}-\frac{\cot\vartheta}{2\beta_{1}^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\,,

(171)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{22}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\beta_{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta_{2}}-\frac{\cot\vartheta}{2\beta_{2}^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\vartheta}\,.

(172)

One gets:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{12}}I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\frac{l}{\Delta_{12}}\,\biggl[(v_{22}-v_{12})I_{j,l+1}-(1-v_{12})I_{j,l}+(1-v_{22})I_{j-1,l+1}\biggr]

(173)

\displaystyle=\frac{j}{\Delta_{12}}\,\biggl[(v_{11}-v_{12})I_{j+1,l}-(1-v_{12})I_{j,l}+(1-v_{11})I_{j+1,l-1}\biggr]\,,

(174)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{11}}I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\frac{j}{2\Delta_{12}}\,\biggl[(v_{22}-v_{12})I_{j+1,l}+(1-v_{22})I_{j,l}-(1-v_{12})I_{j+1,l-1}\biggr]\,,

(175)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{22}}I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\frac{l}{2\Delta_{12}}\,\biggl[(v_{11}-v_{12})I_{j,l+1}+(1-v_{11})I_{j,l}-(1-v_{12})I_{j-1,l+1}\biggr]\,.

(176)

Two independent ways of calculating
\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{12}}I_{j,l}

[see Eqs. (
173
) and (
174
)]
lead to the consistency relation

\displaystyle l(v_{22}-v_{12})I_{j,l+1}-l(1-v_{12})I_{j,l}+l(1-v_{22})I_{j-1,l+1}

(177)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle j(v_{11}-v_{12})I_{j+1,l}-j(1-v_{12})I_{j,l}+j(1-v_{11})I_{j+1,l-1}\,.

This is a first algebraic (recurrence) relation between
I_{j,l}
with different indices.

From Eqs. (
173
)-(
176
)
we deduce the following identities

\displaystyle 2l\,\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{11}}I_{j,l+1}

\displaystyle=j\,\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{12}}I_{j+1,l}\,,

(178)

\displaystyle 2j\,\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{22}}I_{j+1,l}

\displaystyle=l\,\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{12}}I_{j,l+1}\,.

(179)

Combining equations (
178
) and (
179
),
we find that
I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)
obeys the second order
partial differential equation (PDE)

\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial v_{12}^{2}}-4\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial v_{11}\partial v_{22}}\right)I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)=0\,.

(180)

Introducing the light-cone coordinates
v_{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(v_{11}\pm v_{22})

this PDE can be written in the standard form

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial v_{+}^{2}}-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial v_{-}^{2}}-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial v_{12}^{2}}\right)I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{+}+v_{-},v_{+}-v_{-};\varepsilon)=0\,.

(181)

We see that
I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{+}+v_{-},v_{+}-v_{-};\varepsilon)
satisfies
a two-dimensional homogenous wave equation with “time”
v_{+}
and “speed of light”
c=1
.
The “light-cone" is given by
0=v_{+}^{2}-v_{-}^{2}-v_{12}^{2}=v_{11}v_{22}-v_{12}^{2}
.
It is the surface of vanishing symmetric rank-2 Gram determinant

G=-X=v_{11}v_{22}-v_{12}^{2}
.
Since the PDE is independent of
\varepsilon
, it must be fulfilled independently at all orders
in the
\varepsilon
-expansion. Therefore, it could serve as a consistency check
for individual terms in the
\varepsilon
-expansion.

Up until now we only considered the dependence on the indices
j
and
l
.
However, we will see that the dependence of
I_{j,l}
on
\varepsilon
is structurally quite similar.
A good starting point is the representation

\displaystyle I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B(j,l)}\,I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\,\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}x_{1}^{j-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}x_{2}^{l-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\times\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{j+l}{2},\frac{j+l+1}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon,1-x_{1}^{2}v_{11}-x_{2}^{2}v_{22}-2x_{1}x_{2}v_{12}\right)\,.

(182)

Calculating the derivative with respect to
v_{12}
using the identity

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c,x)=\frac{ab}{c}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}(a+1,b+1,c+1,x)\,,

(183)

yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{12}}I_{j,l}(\varepsilon)=-\frac{jl}{1-2\varepsilon}I_{j+1,l+1}(\varepsilon-1)\,.

(184)

Using either Eq. (
173
) or Eq. (
174
)
to express
\partial I_{j,l}/\partial v_{12}

results in an algebraic equation for
I_{j,l}(\varepsilon-1)

in terms of
I_{j^{\prime},l^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)
.
Another way to obtain a formula of this kind is to start from the original angular integral
and to express the additional factor
\sin^{2}\theta_{1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2}
in terms of propagators:

\displaystyle\sin^{2}\theta_{1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{\Delta_{12}}\,\biggl[\beta_{1}^{2}\,L^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)+\beta_{2}^{2}\,L^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)-2\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cos\vartheta\,L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\biggr]

(185)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta_{12}}\,\biggl[v_{11}v_{22}-v_{12}^{2}-(1-v_{11})\Delta^{-2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)-(1-v_{22})\Delta^{-2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(v_{12}-v_{11})\Delta^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)+2(v_{12}-v_{22})\Delta^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(1-v_{12})\Delta^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{-1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\biggr]\,.

Next, we use Eq. (
185
) to obtain the

dimensional recurrence identity

for angular integral
I_{j,l}(\varepsilon)
:

\displaystyle\hskip-35.56593ptI_{j,l}(\varepsilon-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\sin^{2}\theta_{1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2}\,\Delta^{j}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

(186)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Delta_{12}}\,\Big[(v_{11}v_{22}-v_{12}^{2})I_{j,l}(\varepsilon)-(1-v_{11})I_{j,l-2}(\varepsilon)-(1-v_{22})I_{j-2,l}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(v_{12}-v_{11})I_{j,l-1}(\varepsilon)+2(v_{12}-v_{22})I_{j-1,l}(\varepsilon)+2(1-v_{12})I_{j-1,l-1}(\varepsilon)\Big]\,.

Here we take into account that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}(\varepsilon)\,\sin^{2}\theta_{1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2}\equiv\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}(\varepsilon-1)\,.

(187)

Now we are in the position to derive the second

dimensional recurrence identity
, which complements
the identity (
186
).
Doing this we start again with Eq. (
182
) and shift
dimension
\varepsilon\rightarrow\varepsilon+1
. Next,
using the contiguos neighbours relation of the
Gauss hypergeometric function

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c-1,x)=\frac{1}{c-1}\left[a\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}(a+1,b,c,x)-(a-c+1)\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c,x)\right]\,,

(188)

we expand the angular integral
I_{j,l}(\varepsilon+1)
into two terms:

\displaystyle I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon+1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B(j,l)}\,I^{(0)}(\varepsilon+1)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}x_{1}^{j-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}x_{2}^{l-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\times\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{j+l}{2},\frac{j+l+1}{2},\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon,1-w_{12}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B(j,l)}\,I^{(0)}(\varepsilon+1)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{1}x_{1}^{j-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}x_{2}x_{2}^{l-1}\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{1-2\varepsilon}\left[(j+l)\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{j+l+1}{2},\frac{j+l+2}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon,w_{12}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{j+l}{2},\frac{j+l+1}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon,w_{12}\right)\right]\,.

(189)

The second term in Eq. (
3.3.2
) directly converts into
integral
I_{j,l}(\epsilon)
. In case of the first term we apply
the identity for the integrand

\displaystyle\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})\,x_{1}^{m}x_{2}^{n}=\delta(1-x_{1}-x_{2})\,(x_{1}^{m+1}x_{2}^{n}+x_{1}^{m}x_{2}^{n+1})\,,

(190)

to obtain a sum of two angular integrals
I_{j+1,l}
and
I_{j,l+1}
.
Finally, using

I^{(0)}(\varepsilon+1)=\frac{2\pi}{1-2(\varepsilon+1)}=-\frac{1-2\varepsilon}{1+2\varepsilon}I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\,,

(191)

we get

\displaystyle I_{j,l}(\varepsilon+1)=\frac{j+l-1+2\varepsilon}{1+2\varepsilon}I_{j,l}(\varepsilon)-\frac{j}{1+2\varepsilon}I_{j+1,l}(\varepsilon)-\frac{l}{1+2\varepsilon}I_{j,l+1}(\varepsilon)\,.

(192)

The latter identity is the second

dimensional recurrence identity
, which is more
compact than the first one (
186
).

We finalize this section with the derivation of the differential equations for index
and dimension rising operators. In particular,
using the set of identities (
173
)-(
176
)
we introduce
the
index
j/l
rising differential operators

at fixed dimension:

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{1,j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(1-v_{11})\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{11}}-(1-v_{12})\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{12}}+j\,,

(193)

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{2,l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(1-v_{22})\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{22}}-(1-v_{12})\frac{\partial}{\partial v_{12}}+l\,,

(194)

which obey the following
index rising differential equations

:

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{1,j}\,I_{j,l}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle j\,I_{j+1,l}(\varepsilon)\,,

(195)

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{2,l}\,I_{j,l}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l\,I_{j,l+1}(\varepsilon)\,.

(196)

Next, we can sum up Eqs. (
195
) and (
196
), where r.h.s.
is further simplified using identity (
192
):

\displaystyle j\,I_{j+1,l}(\varepsilon)+l\,I_{j,l+1}(\varepsilon)=(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l}(\varepsilon)-(1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l}(\varepsilon+1)\,.

(197)

Now it is convenient to define
the
dimension rising differential operator

at fixed indices:

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{\varepsilon}=\sum\limits_{v=v_{11},v_{12},v_{22}}\!\!\!\!\!2(1-v)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\,-\,1+2\varepsilon\,,

(198)

which obeys the following

dimension rising differential equation
:

\displaystyle\hat{D}_{\varepsilon}\,I_{j,l}(\varepsilon)=(1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l}(\varepsilon+1)\,.

(199)

3.3.3
Partial integration identities

Partial integration identities between angular integrals are another and complementary
possibility to differential identities derived in previous section.
We will perform the partial integration with respect to
\theta_{1}
. We will need

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{1}}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\beta_{1}\sin\theta_{1}\,\Delta^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\,,

(200)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{1}}\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\beta_{2}\,\Big(\cos\vartheta\sin\theta_{1}-\sin\vartheta\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\Big)\,\Delta^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\,.

(201)

Using

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}}\frac{\sin^{2-2\varepsilon}\theta_{1}}{2-2\varepsilon}=\sin^{1-2\varepsilon}\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{1}\,,

(202)

and

\displaystyle 1\equiv(1-\beta_{1}\cos\theta_{1})\,\Delta_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\,,

(203)

it holds

\displaystyle I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\Delta^{j}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=I_{j+1,l}+\frac{\beta_{1}}{2-2\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}\sin^{-2\varepsilon}\theta_{2}\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\sin^{2-2\varepsilon}\theta_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{1}}\left[\Delta^{j+1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\right]\,.

(204)

Making the partial derivative

\displaystyle\hskip-35.56593pt\beta_{1}\sin\theta_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta_{1}}\left[\Delta^{j+1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l\,\Delta^{j+1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l+1}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\Big[L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)L_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)-\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cos\vartheta\biggr]

(205)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(j+1)\,\Delta^{j+2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)\Delta^{l}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{2}}\right)\Big[L^{2}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\mathbf{v_{1}}\right)-\beta_{1}^{2}\Big]\,,

and plugging it into Eq. (
204
) yields

\displaystyle I_{j,l}=I_{j+1,l}+\frac{1}{2-2\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\biggl[\Big(j+1\Big)\Big((1-\beta_{1}^{2})I_{j+2,l}-2I_{j+1,l}+I_{j,l}\Big)

\displaystyle+l\Big((1-\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cos\vartheta)I_{j+1,l+1}-I_{j,l+1}-I_{j+1,l}+I_{j,l}\Big)\biggr]\,.

(206)

Collecting
I_{j,l}
with same indices and using
1-\beta_{1}^{2}=v_{11}
and

1-\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\cos\vartheta=v_{12}
results in the partial integration identity

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l}-(2j+l+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j+1,l}+v_{11}\,(j+1)\,I_{j+2,l}

(207)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle l\,I_{j,l+1}+l\,v_{12}\,I_{j+1,l+1}\,.

Interchanging
\mathbf{v_{1}}
and
\mathbf{v_{2}}
yields the second complementary relation

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l}-(2l+j+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l+1}+v_{22}\,(l+1)\,I_{j,l+2}

(208)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle j\,I_{j+1,l}+j\,v_{12}\,I_{j+1,l+1}\,.

3.3.4
Recursion relations

In Sec.
3.3.2
and
3.3.3
we derived three important
relations (
177
)-(
208
) based on differential and integration properties
of angular integrals:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(j-l)\,(1-v_{12})\,I_{j,l}-j\,(1-v_{11})\,I_{j+1,l-1}+l\,(1-v_{22})\,I_{j-1,l+1}

(209)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle j\,(v_{12}-v_{11})\,I_{j+1,l}-l\,(v_{12}-v_{22})\,I_{j,l+1}\,,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l}-(2j+l+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j+1,l}+v_{11}\,(j+1)\,I_{j+2,l}

(210)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle l\,I_{j,l+1}+l\,v_{12}\,I_{j+1,l+1}\,,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l}-(2l+j+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l+1}+v_{22}\,(l+1)\,I_{j,l+2}

(211)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle j\,I_{j+1,l}+j\,v_{12}\,I_{j+1,l+1}\,.

The first relation is symmetric under exchange
(j,v_{11})\leftrightarrow(l,v_{22})
,
while the second and the third are equivalent under such exchange.
Using these relations one can establish recursion relations to deduce all
angular integrals
I_{j,l}
with
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
. As starting point of the recursion
procedure we use the set of three basic integrals: with no denominators

I_{0,0}=I^{(0)}=2\pi/(1-2\varepsilon)
,
with one massive denominator
I_{1,0}=I_{1,0}^{(1)}(v_{11};\varepsilon)
,
and with two massive denominators
I_{1,1}=I_{1,1}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22})
.

We are now in the position to provide the complete set of recursive relations involving angular
integrals
I_{j,l}
for all
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
. We consider five cases depending on signs of
the indices
j
and
l
.

First case:

l=0
and
j>0
(or
j=0
and
l>0
).

We start with trivial case, when one of the indices is zero and the other is positive.
In case of
l=0
and
j>0
we take the identity (
210
) put
l=0
and shift index
j

as
j\to j-2
. We get the required recursion relation

\displaystyle I_{j,0}=\frac{1}{v_{11}(j-1)}\,\left[2(j-2+\varepsilon)I_{j-1,0}-(j-3+2\varepsilon)I_{j-2,0}\right]\,,

(212)

valid for
j\geq 2
. The starting input for the recursion are two basic (parent) integrals

I_{0,0}
and
I_{1,0}
corresponding to
j=0
and
j=1
, respectively.

Next three integrals for this case are determined by the relations:

\displaystyle I_{2,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v_{11}}\,\left[2\varepsilon\,I_{1,0}+(1-2\varepsilon)\,I_{0,0}\right]\,,

\displaystyle I_{3,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v_{11}}\,\left[(1+\varepsilon)\,I_{2,0}-\varepsilon\,I_{1,0}\right]\,,

\displaystyle I_{4,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3v_{11}}\,\left[2(2+\varepsilon)\,I_{3,0}-(1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{2,0}\right]\,.

(213)

The recursion procedure for
j=0
,
l>0
is completely analogous with
(j,v_{11})
and
(l,v_{22})

interchanged.

For massless case
v_{11}=0
at
l=0
and
j>0
the recursion is again derived from
identity (
210
) by shifting index
j
as
j\to j-1
:

\displaystyle I_{j,0}=\frac{j-2+2\varepsilon}{2j-2+2\varepsilon}\,I_{j-1,0}\,,

(214)

valid for
j\geq 1
and is starting with basic integral
I_{0,0}
.

In this case the next four integrals are fixed from:

\displaystyle I_{1,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{0,0}\,\frac{-1+2\varepsilon}{2\varepsilon}\,,

\displaystyle I_{2,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1,0}\,\frac{\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}\,,

\displaystyle I_{3,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{2,0}\,\frac{1+2\varepsilon}{4+2\varepsilon}\,,

\displaystyle I_{4,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{3,0}\,\frac{1+\varepsilon}{3+\varepsilon}\,.

(215)

The recursion for
j=0
,
l>0
is completely analogous with
j
and
l

interchanged.

In Appendix
G
we present explicit results for the angular integrals
and perform the
\varepsilon
expansion.

Second case:

l=0
and
j<0
(or
j=0
and
l<0
).

In case
l=0
and
j<0
we again use the identity (
210
)
and resort the terms there to get the following
recursion relation:

\displaystyle I_{j,0}=\frac{1}{1-j-2\varepsilon}\,\left[-2(j+\varepsilon)\,I_{j+1,0}+v_{11}(j+1)\,I_{j+2,0}\right]\,,

(216)

starting with
I_{0,0}
and valid for
j\leq-1
.
In particular, the next four integrals are:

\displaystyle I_{-1,0}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle I_{0,0}\,,

\displaystyle I_{-2,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{0,0}\,\frac{4-v_{11}-2\varepsilon}{3-2\varepsilon}\,,

\displaystyle I_{-3,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2-\varepsilon}\Big[-v_{11}I_{0,0}+(3-\varepsilon)I_{-2,0}\Big]\,,

\displaystyle I_{-4,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{5-2\varepsilon}\Big[-3v_{11}I_{-2,0}+2(4-\varepsilon)I_{-3,0}\Big]\,.

(217)

The recursion for
j=0
,
l<0
is completely analogous
with
(j,v_{11})
and
(l,v_{22})
interchanged.

In the massless case (
j<0
,
l=0
,
v_{11}=0
)
the recursion relation is further simplified to

\displaystyle I_{j,0}=-\frac{2(j+\varepsilon)}{1-j-2\varepsilon}\,I_{j+1,0}\,,

(218)

In particular, the results for the next three integrals read

\displaystyle I_{-2,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{-1,0}\,\frac{4-2\varepsilon}{3-2\varepsilon}\,,

\displaystyle I_{-3,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{-2,0}\,\frac{3-\varepsilon}{2-\varepsilon}\,,

\displaystyle I_{-4,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{-3,0}\,\frac{8-2\varepsilon}{5-2\varepsilon}\,.

(219)

The case (
j=0
,
l<0
,
v_{22}=0
) is completely analogous with
j
and
l
interchanged.

Third case:

j<0
and
l<0
.

In the case of no actual denominators (
j<0
and
l<0
)
we can give a closed polynomial formula for
I_{j,l}
.
Using Eqs. (
103
), (
3.2.4
),
(
D.4
), and (
D.4
)
we get

\displaystyle I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\overset{j,l\leq 0}{=}\pi\sum_{m=0}^{-j}\binom{-j}{m}\,(1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}})^{-j-m}\,(1-v_{11})^{m/2}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{-l}\binom{-l}{n}\,(1-\sqrt{1-v_{22}})^{-l-n}\,(1-v_{22})^{n/2}

\displaystyle\times 2^{m+n+1}\,\frac{\left(1-\varepsilon\right)_{m}\left(1-\varepsilon\right)_{n}}{\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)_{m+n+1}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(-m,-n,1-\varepsilon,1-\frac{\hat{v}_{12}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi\sum_{m=0}^{-j}\,\sum_{n=0}^{-l}\sum_{p=0}^{\mathrm{min}(m,n)}\binom{-j}{m}\binom{-l}{n}(1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}})^{-j-m}\,(1-v_{11})^{m/2}

\displaystyle\times(1-\sqrt{1-v_{22}})^{-l-n}\,(1-v_{22})^{n/2}\,2^{m+n+1}\,\frac{\left(1-\varepsilon\right)_{m}\left(1-\varepsilon\right)_{n}}{\left(1-2\varepsilon\right)_{m+n+1}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\left(-m\right)_{p}\left(-n\right)_{p}}{\left(1-\varepsilon\right)_{p}p!}\,\left(1-\frac{\hat{v}_{12}}{2}\right)^{p}\,,

(220)

where

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{12}=\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{1-v_{12}}{\sqrt{1-v_{11}}\sqrt{1-v_{22}}}\right)\,.

(221)

Here the sum over
p
terminates at
\mathrm{min}(m,n)
, because

\left(-m\right)_{p}\,\left(-n\right)_{p}=0
at

p>\mathrm{min}(m,n)
.

To complement the exact result for both negative indices we derive
the recursion relation. It is derived summing two basic
formulas (
210
) and (
211
). One gets:

\displaystyle I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)}\,\biggl[(3l+j+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j,l+1}\,+\,(3j+l+2\varepsilon)\,I_{j+1,l}

(222)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle v_{11}\,(j+1)\,I_{j+2,l}\,-\,v_{22}\,(l+1)\,I_{j,l+2}\,-\,v_{12}\,(j+l)\,I_{j+1,l+1}\biggr]\,.

This recursion relation is valid for all
j,l\leq-1
and
manifestly symmetric under interchange of two sets

(j,v_{11})
and
(l,v_{22})
. E.g., the expressions for
determination of a few first integrals read:

\displaystyle I_{-1,-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3-2\varepsilon}\,\biggl[(2-\varepsilon)\,\Big(I_{-1,0}+I_{0,-1}\Big)-v_{12}I_{0,0}\biggr]=I_{0,0}\,\frac{4-2\varepsilon-v_{12}}{3-2\varepsilon}\,,

\displaystyle I_{-1,-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4(2-\varepsilon)}\,\biggl[(7-2\varepsilon)I_{-1,-1}\,+\,(5-2\varepsilon)I_{0,-2}\,-\,v_{22}\,I_{-1,0}\,-\,3v_{12}\,I_{0,-1}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{0,0}\ \frac{6-2\varepsilon-2v_{12}-v_{22}}{3-2\varepsilon}\,,

\displaystyle I_{-2,-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{5-2\varepsilon}\,\biggl[(4-\varepsilon)\,\Big(I_{-2,-1}+I_{-1,-2}\Big)\,-\,\frac{v_{11}}{2}\,I_{0,-2}\,-\,\frac{v_{22}}{2}\,I_{-2,0}\,-\,2v_{12}\,I_{-1,-1}\bigg]

(223)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{I_{0,0}}{(3-2\varepsilon)(5-2\varepsilon)}\,\biggl[(3-\varepsilon)\Big(4-\varepsilon-\frac{v_{11}+v_{22}+4v_{22}}{2}\Big)+v_{11}v_{22}+2v_{12}^{2}\biggr]\,.

In Appendix
G
we display the integrals for
-4\leq j,l\leq-1

and perform their
\varepsilon
expansion up to order
\varepsilon
.
Note, that the limit of massless particles
v_{11}=0
or/and

v_{22}=0
is straightforward in this case.

Fourth case:

j>0
and
l<0
(or
l>0
,
j<0
).

For the case, that the indices of the angular integral have opposite signs

j>0
and
l<0
(or
l>0
,
j<0
) we involve all three
master identities (
209
)-(
211
). In particular,
in case
j>0
and
l<0
we do the following:
(1) first, we shift indices
j\to j-1
in (
210
)
and
l\to l-1
in (
211
); (2) sum three equations
multiplying each of them with
-1
,
-(1-v_{12})
, and
(1-v_{11})
,
respectively. After shifting index
l\to l+1
in the resulting expression
we get the recursive relation

\displaystyle I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-v_{12}}{1-v_{11}}\,I_{j-1,l+1}\,+\,\frac{1}{(1-v_{11})\,(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)}\,\biggl[I_{j-1,l+2}\,(l+1)\,(v_{12}-v_{22})

(224)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle I_{j,l+2}\,(l+1)\,X\,+\,I_{j,l+1}\,(j+2l+2\varepsilon)\,(v_{12}-v_{11})\biggr]\,,

where
X=v_{12}^{2}-v_{11}v_{22}
.
Starting integrals for this recursion, which are known to us,
are
I_{0,0}
,
I_{1,0}
, and
I_{0,1}
. The integral
I_{1,1}

does not appear in this recursion since
(l+1)I_{j,l+2}=0

at
l=-1
.

We present a few examples of the expressions for the angular
integrals with
j>0
and
l<0
:

\displaystyle I_{1,-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-v_{12}}{1-v_{11}}\,I_{0,0}\,+\,\frac{v_{12}-v_{11}}{1-v_{11}}\,I_{1,0}\,,

\displaystyle I_{1,-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-v_{12}}{1-v_{11}}\,I_{0,-1}\,+\,\frac{1}{2(1-v_{11})(1-\varepsilon)}\,\biggl[(v_{12}-v_{22})\,I_{0,0}\,-\,X\,I_{1,0}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(v_{12}-v_{11})(3-2\varepsilon)\,I_{1,-1}\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle I_{2,-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-v_{12}}{1-v_{11}}\,I_{1,-1}\,+\,\frac{1}{(1-v_{11})(1-2\varepsilon)}\,\biggl[(v_{12}-v_{22})\,I_{1,0}\,-\,X\,I_{2,0}

(225)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(v_{12}-v_{11})(1-\varepsilon)\,I_{2,-1}\biggr]\,.

By analogy we can derive the recursive relation
for the case with
j<0
and
l>0
making
interchange of two sets

(j,v_{11})
and
(l,v_{22})
:

\displaystyle I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-v_{12}}{1-v_{22}}\,I_{j+1,l-1}\,+\,\frac{1}{(1-v_{22})\,(j+l-1+2\varepsilon)}\,\biggl[I_{j+2,l-1}\,(j+1)\,(v_{12}-v_{11})

(226)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle I_{j+2,l}\,(j+1)\,X\,+\,I_{j+1,l}\,(2j+l+2\varepsilon)\,(v_{12}-v_{22})\biggr]\,.

Fifth case:

j>0
and
l>0
.

To derive the recursive relations for the

j>0
and
l>0
case we start with Eqs. (
224
), (
226
)
and shift the indices there as

l\to l-2
in Eq. (
224
) and

j\to j-2
in Eq. (
226
). We get two
recursive relations

\displaystyle I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{X(j-1)}\,\biggl[I_{j-1,l}\,(v_{12}-v_{22})\,(2j+l-4+2\varepsilon)\,-\,I_{j-2,l}\,(1-v_{22})\,(j+l-3+2\varepsilon)

(227)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle I_{j,l-1}\,(j-1)\,(v_{12}-v_{11})\,+\,I_{j-1,l-1}\,(j+l-3+2\varepsilon)\,(1-v_{12})\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{X(l-1)}\,\biggl[I_{j,l-1}\,(v_{12}-v_{11})\,(2l+j-4+2\varepsilon)\,-\,I_{j,l-2}\,(1-v_{11})\,(j+l-3+2\varepsilon)

(228)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle I_{j-1,l}\,(l-1)\,(v_{12}-v_{22})\,+\,I_{j-1,l-1}\,(j+l-3+2\varepsilon)\,(1-v_{12})\biggr]\,,

valid for
j\geq 2
,
l\geq 1
and
j\geq 1
,
l\geq 2
and starting
with basic integral
I_{1,1}
.
One can see that Eq. (
227
) is not valid for
j=1,l=2
,
while Eq. (
228
) is not valid for
j=2,l=1
because of a pole.
To avoid the pole and to get the recursive relation, which is manifestly
symmetric under interchange of
(j,v_{11})
and
(l,v_{22})

we simply multiply Eq. (
227
) with factor
X(j-1)
and Eq. (
228
)
with factor
X(l-1)
and sum them up. As a result we
get the recursive relation, which is more suitable for our purposes

\displaystyle I_{j,l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{j+l-3+2\varepsilon}{X(j+l-2)}\,\biggl[2(1-v_{12})\,I_{j-1,l-1}\,-\,(1-v_{11})\,I_{j,l-2}\,-\,(1-v_{22})\,I_{j-2,l}\biggr]

(229)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2j+2l-5+2\varepsilon}{X(j+l-2)}\,\biggl[(v_{12}-v_{11})\,I_{j,l-1}\,+\,(v_{12}-v_{22})\,I_{j-1,l}\biggr]\,,

valid for all
j,l\geq 1
and
j+l\neq 2
. Note, that
j+l\neq 2
means that

j=l=1
and this choice corresponds to the master integral
I_{1,1}
.

Below we list few examples of integrals with positive
j
and
l
:

\displaystyle I_{1,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{X}\,\biggl[(v_{12}-v_{11})(1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{1,1}-2(1-v_{11})\,\varepsilon\,I_{1,0}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(1-v_{12})\,\varepsilon\,I_{0,1}+(v_{12}-v_{22})\,I_{0,2}\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle I_{2,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{X}\,\biggl[(v_{12}-v_{22})(1+2\varepsilon)\,I_{1,1}-2(1-v_{22})\,\varepsilon\,I_{0,1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(1-v_{12})\,\varepsilon\,I_{1,0}+(v_{12}-v_{11})\,I_{2,0}\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle I_{2,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1+2\varepsilon}{2X}\,\biggl[2(1-v_{12})\,I_{1,1}-(1-v_{11})\,I_{2,0}-(1-v_{22})\,I_{0,2}\biggr]

(230)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{3+2\varepsilon}{2X}\,\biggl[(v_{12}-v_{11})\,I_{2,1}+(v_{12}-v_{22})\,I_{1,2}\biggr]\,.

Finally, we stress that the main result of this section is the derivation of the set
of recursive relations [see Eqs. (
212
),
(
214
),
(
216
),
(
218
),
(
222
),
(
224
),
(
226
),
and
(
229
)], which together with three
master integrals
I_{0,0}
,
I_{1,0}
, and
I_{1,1}
can deduce any angular

I_{j,l}
with arbitrary indices
j
and
l
.
Therefore, the
\varepsilon
-expansion of any
I_{j,l}
follows from expansion
of master integrals
I_{0,0}
,
I_{1,0}
, and
I_{1,1}
.
The recursion relations have been implemented together with the all-order expansions of the basis
integrals in
Mathematica
.
This allows for fast calculation of any
I_{j,l}
with
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
to arbitrary order
in
\varepsilon
.

3.4
All order
\varepsilon
-expansion of angular integrals

The hypergeometric representations of the angular integrals derived
in Sec.
3.2
allow for
a systematic
\varepsilon
-expansion up to desired order. In the cases where the angular integrals are expressible
in terms of the Gauss hypergeometric function
there are publicly available algorithms for the
\varepsilon
-expansion up to considerably
high order. A well-suited Mathematica-based package is
HypExp

Huber:2005yg
, which provides
analytic expansions in terms of harmonic polylogarithms.

In this chapter we derive the analytical formalism allowing for
the all order
\varepsilon
-expansions for the integrals up to two denominators (i.e.

the basic angular integrals
) in terms of the single and double
Nielsen polylogarithms
Kolbig:1969zza
;
Kolbig:1983qt
.
As it was shown
in Sec.
3.2.6
for arbitrary dimension
D

the double massive angular integral can be transformed to a finite sum of
single-massive integrals based on the reduction formalism proposed by us. Therefore,

\varepsilon
-expansion of the double massive integral for any
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
can be generated
by use of the
\varepsilon
-expansion of the single massive integral.
Using two mass splitting (see Sec.
3.2.6
)
and recursion relations (see Sec.
3.3.4
)
any angular integral
I_{j,l}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

with
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
can be expressed in terms of the basic angular integrals
mentioned above.
Note, that these results have never been discussed or derived before in literature.
Only expansion up to a few first orders in
\varepsilon
were known before
(see, e.g. results in Refs.
vanNeerven:1985xr
;
Beenakker:1988bq
;
Somogyi:2011ir
).
Therefore, we think that our findings will be useful as input for the calculation
of QCD processes.

3.4.1
Massive integral with one denominator

We start with the hypergeometric representation for the massive integral
with one denominator (
102
)

\displaystyle I_{1}^{(1)}(v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{(1-\beta)}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,1-\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,-\frac{2\beta}{1-\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{1-2\varepsilon}\frac{1}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,1-\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,1-\frac{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)\,.

(231)

The
\varepsilon
-expansion of the
\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1-\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,x)
is established the following way.
First, we perform the Euler transformation and employ the Euler integral representation for
the Gauss hypergeometric function
\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}
:

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1-\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=(1-x)^{-\varepsilon}\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}(1-\varepsilon,1-2\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=(1-x)^{-\varepsilon}(1-2\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{-2\varepsilon}(1-xt)^{-1+\varepsilon}\,.

(232)

Next using
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon t(1-xt)^{-1+\varepsilon}
we get

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1-\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-2\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}(1-x)^{-\varepsilon}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,t^{-2\varepsilon}(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}\,.

(233)

To separate the pole at
t=0
in Eq. (
3.4.1
) we use the decomposition

\displaystyle(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}=1+[(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}-1]

(234)

and drop the first constant term,
since it vanishes when differentiated w.r.t.
x
.
Then we perform the
\varepsilon
expansion of the remaining
(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}-1
. It yields

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1-\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1-2\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}(1-x)^{-\varepsilon}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-2)^{n}}{n!m!}\varepsilon^{m+n}

(235)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,\log^{n}t\log^{m}(1-xt)\,.

The latter expansion can be written in a more compact form using
the familiar
Nielsen polylogarithms

Kolbig:1969zza
;
Kolbig:1983qt

\displaystyle S_{n,p}(x)\equiv\frac{(-1)^{n+p-1}}{(n-1)!p!}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,\log^{n-1}t\log^{p}(1-xt)\,,

(236)

which satisfies the differential equation

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}S_{n,p}(x)=\frac{1}{x}S_{n-1,p}(x)\,.

(237)

Hence, we have

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1-\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-2\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}(1-x)^{-\varepsilon}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{n}(-1)^{m}\varepsilon^{m+n}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}S_{n+1,m}(x)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-2\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}\frac{(1-x)^{-\varepsilon}}{x}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{n}(-1)^{m}\varepsilon^{m+n}S_{n,m}(x)\,.

(238)

Next, rearranging the summation to make it over
N=m+n-1

and shifting
m\rightarrow m+1
yields

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1-\varepsilon,2-2\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=(1-2\varepsilon)\frac{(1-x)^{-\varepsilon}}{x}\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{N}2^{N-m}(-1)^{m}S_{N-m,m+1}(x)\,\varepsilon^{N}\,.

(239)

Therefore, we have established the all order
\varepsilon
-expansion of the
massive integral with one denominator (
102
)

\displaystyle I_{1}^{(1)}(v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\left(\frac{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)^{-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{N}2^{N-m}(-1)^{m+1}S_{N-m,m+1}\left(1-\frac{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)\,\varepsilon^{N}\,.

(240)

Using specific Nielsen polylogarithms

S_{0,p}(x)=(-1)^{p}\log^{p}(1-x)/p!
and
S_{n,1}(x)=\mathrm{Li}_{n+1}(x)

this explicitly gives up to order
\varepsilon

\displaystyle I_{1}^{(1)}(v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\left(\frac{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)^{-\varepsilon}\left[\log\left(\frac{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)-2\varepsilon\,\mathrm{Li}_{2}\left(1-\frac{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})\right]\,.

(241)

The latter formula is exactly the same expansion as obtained in Ref.
Somogyi:2011ir
.

Eq. (
3.4.1
) together with the recursion relation (
212
)
completes all order
\varepsilon
-expansion of the angular integrals
I^{(1)}_{j}(v_{11};\varepsilon)

with one massive denominator.
In particular, starting from
I_{0}^{(1)}(v_{11};\varepsilon)\equiv I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)=2\pi/(1-2\varepsilon)

and
I_{1}^{(1)}(v_{11};\varepsilon)
given by Eq. (
3.4.1
) we can derive
analytical results for any power of massive denominator
j
. For example,
the results for
j=2,3,4
are given by the recursion identities:

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{2}(v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{v_{11}}\,\biggl[2\varepsilon I^{(1)}_{1}(v_{11};\varepsilon)+(1-2\varepsilon)I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{3}(v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2v_{11}}\,\biggl[2(1+\varepsilon)I^{(1)}_{2}(v_{11};\varepsilon)-2\varepsilon I^{(1)}_{1}(v_{11};\varepsilon)\biggr]\,,

(242)

\displaystyle I^{(1)}_{4}(v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3v_{11}}\,\biggl[2(2+\varepsilon)I^{(1)}_{3}(v_{11};\varepsilon)-(1+2\varepsilon)I^{(2)}_{2}(v_{11};\varepsilon)\biggr]\,.

3.4.2
Massless integral with two denominators

For the two denominator massless integral we start with the
hypergeometric representation (
3.2.4
)

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(0)}(v_{12};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=I^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\frac{\left(-2\varepsilon\right)_{2}}{2^{2}\left(-\varepsilon\right)_{1}\left(-\varepsilon\right)_{1}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,1,1-\varepsilon,1-\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,1,1-\varepsilon,1-\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)\,.

(243)

The
\varepsilon
-expansion of the
\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,1,1-\varepsilon,x\right)
runs analogous to the
one denominator one mass case considered above. First, we perform the Euler transformation
and employ the Euler integral representation of the
\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}
:

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=(1-x)^{-1-\varepsilon}\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}(-\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=(1-x)^{-1-\varepsilon}(-\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{-\varepsilon-1}(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}\,.

(244)

The pole at
t=0
is taken care of by subtracting the numerator at the pole.
Afterwards we can safely expand in
\varepsilon
.

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon(1-x)^{-1-\varepsilon}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,t^{-\varepsilon}\left[(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}-1\right]+\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{-\varepsilon-1}\right)

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon(1-x)^{-1-\varepsilon}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\varepsilon)^{n}}{n!}\log^{n}t\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{m}}{m!}\log^{m}(1-xt)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)\,.

(245)

Rearranging the terms and evaluating the logarithmic integrals in terms of the
Nielsen polylogarithms yields

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(1-x)^{-1-\varepsilon}\left[1-\varepsilon\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!m!}\varepsilon^{m+n}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,\log^{n}t\log^{m}(1-xt)\right]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(1-x)^{-1-\varepsilon}\left[1-\varepsilon\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{m}\varepsilon^{m+n}S_{n+1,m}(x)\right]\,.

(246)

Performing the summation over
N=m+n+1
and
m
gives

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,1,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=(1-x)^{-1-\varepsilon}\left[1-\sum_{N=2}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{N-1}(-1)^{m}S_{N-m,m}(x)\,\varepsilon^{N}\right]\,.

(247)

Therefore we have established the all order expansion

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(0)}(v_{12},\varepsilon)=\pi\left(\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)^{-1-\varepsilon}\left[-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{N}(-1)^{m}S_{N-m+1,m}\left(1-\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)\,\varepsilon^{N}\right]\,.

(248)

For checking purposes we explicitly write out the terms up to order
\varepsilon
and
confirm that it exactly coincides with result derived
before in Refs.
vanNeerven:1985xr
;
Beenakker:1988bq
;
Somogyi:2011ir
:

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(0)}(v_{12},\varepsilon)=\pi\left(\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)^{-1-\varepsilon}\left[-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}-\varepsilon\,\mathrm{Li}_{2}\left(1-\frac{v_{12}}{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})\right]\,.

(249)

3.4.3
Single massive integral with two denominators

For the two denominator single massive integral we start with the
hypergeometric representation (
3.2.5
)

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(1)}(v_{12},v_{11};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{I_{1}^{(0)}(\varepsilon)}{v_{12}}\left(\frac{v_{11}}{v^{2}_{12}}\right)^{\varepsilon}F_{1}\left(-2\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,1-2\varepsilon,\omega_{+},\omega_{-}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\omega_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{\pm}}{1-\tau_{\pm}}=1-\frac{v_{12}}{1\pm\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\,,

(250)

where
I_{1}^{(0)}(\varepsilon)=-\pi/\varepsilon
.
The
\varepsilon
-expansion of the Appell function
F_{1}(-2\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,1-2\varepsilon,x,y)

is established similarly to the previous cases. Employing the
Euler integral representation and separating the pole gives

\displaystyle F_{1}(-2\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,1-2\varepsilon,x,y)

\displaystyle=-2\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{-1-2\varepsilon}(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}(1-yt)^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=-2\varepsilon\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,t^{-2\varepsilon}\left[(1-xt)^{\varepsilon}(1-yt)^{\varepsilon}-1\right]+\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{-1-2\varepsilon}\right)\,.

(251)

Now we can safely expand in
\varepsilon
under the integral to obtain

\displaystyle F_{1}(\dots)

\displaystyle=-2\varepsilon\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-2\varepsilon)^{n}}{n!}\log^{n}t\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{m}}{m!}\log^{m}\left((1-xt)(1-yt)\right)-\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=1-2\varepsilon\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-2)^{n}}{n!m!}\varepsilon^{m+n}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\log^{n}t\log^{m}\left((1-xt)(1-yt)\right)\,.

(252)

By binomial expansion the
\log^{m}
term can be written in the form

\displaystyle\log^{m}\left((1-xt)(1-yt)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\log(1-xt)+\log(1-yt)\right)^{m}

(253)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{m}\binom{m}{k}\log^{m-k}(1-xt)\log^{k}(1-yt)\,.

Hence we can write

\displaystyle F_{1}(\dots)

\displaystyle=1-2\varepsilon\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-2)^{n}}{n!m!}\varepsilon^{m+n}\sum_{k=0}^{m}\binom{m}{k}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\log^{n}t\log^{m-k}(1-xt)\log^{k}(1-yt)\,.

(254)

The logarithmic integral is the natural two-variable generalization of the Nielsen polylogarithm.
Defining the
double Nielsen polylogarithm

[see discussion of their properties in Appendix
F
]

\displaystyle S_{n,p_{1},p_{2}}(x,y)\equiv\frac{(-1)^{n+p_{1}+p_{2}-1}}{(n-1)!p_{1}!p_{2}!}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,\log^{n-1}t\log^{p_{1}}(1-xt)\log^{p_{2}}(1-yt)\,,

(255)

we can evaluate the integrals to

\displaystyle F_{1}(\dots)

\displaystyle=1-2\varepsilon\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{m}2^{n}\varepsilon^{m+n}\sum_{k=0}^{m}S_{n+1,m-k,k}(x,y)\,.

(256)

Changing summation to run over
N=m+n+1
and
m
yields the result

\displaystyle F_{1}(-2\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,-\varepsilon,1-2\varepsilon,x,y)

\displaystyle=1-\sum_{N=2}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{N-1}(-1)^{m}2^{N-m}\sum_{k=0}^{m}S_{N-m,m-k,k}(x,y)\,\varepsilon^{N}\,.

(257)

This establishes the all-order expansion of the single massive
two denominator integral in the form

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(1)}(v_{12},v_{11},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{v_{12}}\biggl(\frac{v_{11}}{v^{2}_{12}}\biggr)^{\varepsilon}\biggl[-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}

(258)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{N}(-1)^{m}2^{N-m+1}\sum_{k=0}^{m}S_{N-m+1,m-k,k}(\tau_{+},\tau_{-})\,\varepsilon^{N}\biggr]\,.

Up to order
\varepsilon
this expression is

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(1)}(v_{12},v_{11},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{v_{12}}\biggl(\frac{v_{11}}{v^{2}_{12}}\biggr)^{\varepsilon}\,\biggl[-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}-2\varepsilon\biggl(\mathrm{Li}_{2}\biggl(1-\frac{v_{12}}{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\biggr)

(259)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathrm{Li}_{2}\biggl(1-\frac{v_{12}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\biggr)\biggr)+\,\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})\vphantom{\frac{1}{1}}\biggr]\,.

Genuine double Nielsen polylogarithms start appearing at order
\varepsilon^{2}
.

3.4.4
Double massive integral with two denominators

As we showed in Sec. (
3.2.6
)
the double massive integral reduces to a sum of single massive
integrals using two mass splitting
[see details in Eqs. (
136
)
and (
136
)]. In particular,
there are two fully equivalent decompositions of the double mass integral
governed by the mixing parameter
\lambda
. Due to rotational invariance
both solutions for the
\lambda
parameter and correspondingly for the
scalar products of the velocities
v_{13}
and
v_{23}
are equivalent
to each other. Hence, we restrict to one of them, e.g. to

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{11}-v_{12}-\sqrt{X}}{v_{11}+v_{22}-2v_{12}}\,,

(260)

\displaystyle v_{13}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{11}\left(v_{22}+\sqrt{X}\right)-v_{12}\left(v_{12}+\sqrt{X}\right)}{v_{11}+v_{22}-2v_{12}}\,,

(261)

\displaystyle v_{23}

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{22}\left(v_{11}-\sqrt{X}\right)-v_{12}\left(v_{12}-\sqrt{X}\right)}{v_{11}+v_{22}-2v_{12}}\,,

(262)

where
X=v_{12}^{2}-v_{11}v_{22}
.

In this case the expression for the
double massive integral
I_{1,1}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

reads:

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}\left[v_{13}I_{1,1}^{(1)}(v_{13},v_{11};\varepsilon)-v_{23}I_{1,1}^{(1)}(v_{23},v_{22};\varepsilon)\right]\,.

(263)

Here we used the relations
\lambda=v_{13}/\sqrt{X}
and
1-\lambda=-v_{23}/\sqrt{X}
.
After cancelling the pole and some rearrangement one gets

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{X}}\left[\log\left(\frac{v_{12}+\sqrt{X}}{v_{12}-\sqrt{X}}\right)-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\varepsilon^{n}}{(n+1)!}\left(\log^{n+1}\frac{v_{11}}{v^{2}_{13}}-\log^{n+1}\frac{v_{22}}{v^{2}_{23}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{v_{11}}{v^{2}_{13}}\right)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{N}(-1)^{m}2^{N-m+1}\sum_{k=0}^{m}S_{N-m+1,m-k,k}(\tau_{+}^{13},\tau_{-}^{13})\,\varepsilon^{N}

\displaystyle\left.-\left(\frac{v_{22}}{v^{2}_{23}}\right)^{\varepsilon}\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=1}^{N}(-1)^{m}2^{N-m+1}\sum_{k=0}^{m}S_{N-m+1,m-k,k}(\tau_{+}^{23},\tau_{-}^{23})\,\varepsilon^{N}\right]\,,

(264)

with
\tau_{\pm}^{13}=1-{v_{13}}/({1\pm\sqrt{1-v_{11}}})
and

\tau_{\pm}^{23}=1-{v_{23}}/({1\pm\sqrt{1-v_{11}}})
.
In derivation of Eq. (
3.4.4
) we use the identity:

\displaystyle\log\frac{v_{22}}{v^{2}_{23}}-\log\frac{v_{11}}{v^{2}_{13}}=\log\left(\frac{v_{12}+\sqrt{X}}{v_{12}-\sqrt{X}}\right)\,.

(265)

Explicitly, the
\varepsilon
expansion up to second order reads:

\displaystyle I_{1,1}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22};\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}\left[v_{13}\,I^{(1)}_{1,1}(v_{13},v_{11};\varepsilon)-v_{23}\,I^{(1)}_{1,1}(v_{23},v_{22};\varepsilon)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{X}}\left[\log\left(\frac{v_{12}+\sqrt{X}}{v_{12}-\sqrt{X}}\right)-\varepsilon\left(\frac{1}{2}\log^{2}\frac{v_{11}}{v_{13}^{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\log^{2}\frac{v_{22}}{v_{23}^{2}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+2\,\mathrm{Li}_{2}\left(1-\frac{v_{13}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)+2\,\mathrm{Li}_{2}\left(1-\frac{v_{13}}{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\left.\left.-2\,\mathrm{Li}_{2}\left(1-\frac{v_{23}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{22}}}\right)-2\,\mathrm{Li}_{2}\left(1-\frac{v_{23}}{1+\sqrt{1-v_{22}}}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^{2})\,.

(266)

3.5
Neerven integrals

In this section we would like to discuss some aspects of the Neerven angular
integrals:

1.

connection between
the Neerven
vanNeerven:1985xr
;
Beenakker:1988bq

and Somogyi
Somogyi:2011ir
parametrization of
angular integrals;

2.

some important remarks on massless and
double massive Neerven integrals;

3.

comment on Mirkes’s recursive formula
Mirkes:1992hu

for a special class of single massive Neerven integrals.

3.5.1
Connection between Neerven and Somogyi parametrization

Discussion of angular integrals in the literature revolves for the most part around results
for integrals of the form

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{D}(a,b,A,B,C)=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{1}{(a+b\cos\theta_{1})^{j}(A+B\cos\theta_{1}+C\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2})^{l}}\,.

(267)

In this section we will review how these angular integrals presented in the Neerven form can
be translated to those in the Somogyi form

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{1}{(v_{1}\cdot k)^{j}(v_{2}\cdot k)^{l}}\,,

(268)

introduced in Sec.
3.2
.
Defining

\displaystyle v_{1}=\left(1,\mathbf{0}_{D-3},0,-\frac{b}{a}\right)\,\,,\qquad v_{2}=\left(1,\mathbf{0}_{D-3},-\frac{C}{A},-\frac{B}{A}\right)\,,

(269)

we have

\displaystyle v_{11}=1-\frac{b^{2}}{a^{2}}\,,\quad v_{22}=1-\frac{B^{2}+C^{2}}{A^{2}}\,,\quad v_{12}=1-\frac{bB}{aA}\,.

(270)

With
k=(1,\dots,\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2},\cos\theta_{1})
we can write the generic Neerven integral as

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{D}(a,b,A,B,C)=\frac{1}{a^{j}A^{l}}\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{1}{(v_{1}\cdot k)^{j}(v_{2}\cdot k)^{l}}\,.

(271)

For the four classes of the Neerven integrals this implies

Massless case:
a^{2}=b^{2}
and
A^{2}=B^{2}+C^{2}

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(a,b,A,B,C)=\frac{1}{a^{j}A^{l}}I^{(0)}_{j,l}\left(1-\frac{bB}{aA};\varepsilon\right)\,.

(272)

Single massive case:
a^{2}=b^{2}

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(a,b,A,B,C)=\frac{1}{a^{j}A^{l}}I^{(1)}_{j,l}\left(1-\frac{bB}{aA},1-\frac{B^{2}+C^{2}}{A^{2}};\varepsilon\right)\,.

(273)

Single massive case:
A^{2}=B^{2}+C^{2}

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(a,b,A,B,C)=\frac{1}{a^{j}A^{l}}I^{(1)}_{j,l}\left(1-\frac{bB}{aA},1-\frac{b^{2}}{a^{2}};\varepsilon\right)\,.

(274)

Double massive case:
a^{2}\neq b^{2}
and
A^{2}\neq B^{2}+C^{2}
:
