\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(a,b,A,B,C)=\frac{1}{a^{j}A^{l}}I^{(2)}_{j,l}\left(1-\frac{bB}{aA},1-\frac{b^{2}}{a^{2}},1-\frac{B^{2}+C^{2}}{A^{2}};\varepsilon\right)\,.

(275)

Note, that there are the following limits:

1.

I^{(2)}_{j,l}
reduces to
I^{(1)}_{j,l}
at
v_{11}=0
or
v_{22}=0
,

2.

I^{(1)}_{j,l}
reduces to
I^{(0)}_{j,l}
at
v_{11}=0
.

Therefore, the integral
I^{(2)}_{j,l}
can be regarded as the
basic Neerven integral (
1
) encoding information
about the others.
Applying the result from Sec.
3.3.1

we can write down the general Neerven integral in hypergeometric form in terms of
the Lauricella function
F_{B}^{(3)}
:

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(a,b,A,B,C)=\frac{1}{a^{j}A^{l}}\frac{2\pi}{1-2\varepsilon}\left(1-\frac{bB}{aA}\right)^{1-j-l-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\times F_{B}^{(3)}\left(\frac{j}{2},\frac{l}{2},\frac{3-j-l-2\varepsilon}{2},\frac{j+1}{2},\frac{l+1}{2},\frac{2-j-l-2\varepsilon}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon;x,y,z\right)\,,

(276)

with the abbreviations
x=\frac{b(Ab-aB)}{a(aA-bB)}
,

y=\frac{a(B^{2}+C^{2})-AbB}{A(aA-bB)}
,
and
z=\frac{bB}{aA}
.

For
j,l\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
we also derive the closed formula for
the angular integral using results from Sec.
3.2

in terms of the Appell function
F_{1}

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(a,b,A,B,C)=\frac{1}{a^{j}A^{l}}\frac{2\pi}{1-2\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\times\biggl[\sum_{n=0}^{j-1}\binom{l-1+n}{l-1}\frac{\lambda_{\pm}^{l}(1-\lambda_{\pm})^{n}(2-l-n-2\varepsilon)_{l+n}}{(v_{13}^{\pm})^{j-n}2^{l+n}(1-l-n-\varepsilon)_{l+n}}\left(\frac{v_{11}}{v_{13}^{\pm 2}}\right)^{l+n-1+\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\times F_{1}\left(2-j-l-2\varepsilon,1-l-n-\varepsilon,1-l-n-\varepsilon,2-l-n-2\varepsilon,x_{13}^{\pm},y_{13}^{\pm}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{l-1}\binom{j-1+n}{j-1}\frac{\lambda_{\pm}^{n}(1-\lambda_{\pm})^{j}(2-l-n-2\varepsilon)_{j+n}}{(v_{23}^{\pm})^{l-n}2^{j+n}(1-j-n-\varepsilon)_{j+n}}\left(\frac{v_{22}}{v_{23}^{\pm 2}}\right)^{j+n-1+\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\times F_{1}\Big(2-j-l-2\varepsilon,1-j-n-\varepsilon,1-j-n-\varepsilon,2-j-n-2\varepsilon,x_{23}^{\pm},y_{23}^{\pm}\Big)\vphantom{\frac{v}{v}^{2}}\biggr]\,,

(277)

where the following notations are used:

\displaystyle v_{11}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{b^{2}}{a^{2}}\,,\,\,v_{22}=1-\frac{B^{2}+C^{2}}{A^{2}}\,,\,\,v_{12}=1-\frac{bB}{aA}\,,\,\,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\frac{A\left(a-abB+Ab^{2}\pm\sqrt{(Ab-aB)^{2}+C^{2}(a-b)(a+b)}\right)}{a^{2}C^{2}+(Ab-aB)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle v_{13}^{\pm}

\displaystyle=(1-\lambda_{\pm})v_{11}+\lambda_{\pm}v_{12}\,,\,\,v_{23}^{\pm}=(1-\lambda_{\pm})v_{12}+\lambda_{\pm}v_{22}\,,\,\,

\displaystyle x_{13}^{\pm}=1-\frac{v_{13}^{\pm}}{1+\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\,,\,\,y_{13}^{\pm}=1-\frac{v_{13}^{\pm}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{11}}}\,,\,\,

\displaystyle x_{23}^{\pm}=1-\frac{v_{23}^{\pm}}{1+\sqrt{1-v_{22}}}\,,\,\,y_{23}^{\pm}=1-\frac{v_{23}^{\pm}}{1-\sqrt{1-v_{22}}}\,.

(278)

3.5.2
On massless integral in the Neerven parametrization

For a check of consistency, we want to derive the massless
Neerven integral in its most commonly used form.
This concerns the integral (compare with Ref.
vanNeerven:1985xr
)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\,\frac{1}{(1-\cos\theta_{1})^{j}(1-\cos\vartheta\cos\theta_{1}-\sin\vartheta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2})^{l}}\,.

(279)

In the notation we have established, we write Eq. (
279
) as

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(1,-1,1,-\cos\vartheta,-\sin\vartheta)=I^{(0)}_{j,l}(1-\cos\vartheta;\varepsilon)\,.

(280)

employing the result (
116
)
and rewriting the cosine with the half-angle formula we obtain

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(1,-1,1,-\cos\vartheta,-\sin\vartheta)=I_{j+l}^{(0)}(\varepsilon)\frac{(1-j-l-\varepsilon)_{j}}{(1-j-\varepsilon)_{j}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(jl,1-\varepsilon,\cos^{2}\frac{\vartheta}{2}\right)\,.

(281)

Writing down
I_{j+l}^{(0)}(\varepsilon)
and the
Pochhammer symbols in terms of
gamma functions we arrive
at the well known result (see, e.g., Ref.
Gordon:1993qc
)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{1}{(1-\cos\theta_{1})^{j}(1-\cos\vartheta\cos\theta_{1}-\sin\vartheta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2})^{l}}

\displaystyle=2^{1-j-l}\pi\,\frac{\Gamma(1-2\varepsilon)}{\Gamma^{2}(1-\varepsilon)}\,B(1-j-\varepsilon,1-l-\varepsilon)\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(j,l,1-\varepsilon,\cos^{2}\frac{\vartheta}{2}\right)\,.

(282)

3.5.3
On massive integral in the Neerven parametrization

To derive the four dimensional result for the double massive integral the proposed method via
two-mass splitting is of course unnecessarily involved. One can instead employ
Weierstrass substitution

\xi=\tan(\theta/2)
on both integrals in Eq. (
1
) and obtain,
by taking advantage of the trigonometric identities

\displaystyle\cos x=\frac{1-\tan^{2}\frac{x}{2}}{1+\tan^{2}\frac{x}{2}}\,,\qquad\sin x=\frac{2\tan\frac{x}{2}}{1+\tan^{2}\frac{x}{2}}\,,

(283)

the integral representation

\displaystyle I^{(j,l)}_{4-2\varepsilon}(a,b,A,B,C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{3-4\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\xi_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}

(284)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{(1+\xi_{1}^{2})^{j+l-2+2\varepsilon}(1+\xi_{2}^{2})^{l-1+2\varepsilon}\xi_{1}^{1-2\varepsilon}\xi_{2}^{-2\varepsilon}}{\Delta^{j}(a,b,\xi_{1})\Delta^{l}(A,B,C,\xi_{1},\xi_{2})}\,,

where

\displaystyle\Delta(a,b,\xi_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a+b+(a-b)\xi_{1}^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\Delta(A,B,C,\xi_{1},\xi_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(A+B+(A-B)\xi_{1}^{2}\Big)(1+\xi_{2}^{2})+2C\xi_{1}^{2}(1-\xi_{2}^{2})\,.

(285)

For
\varepsilon=0
and
j=l=1
this vastly simplifies and can be elementary integrated
to yield (compare, e.g., with Ref.
Schellekens:1981kq
)

\displaystyle I^{(1,1)}_{4}(a,b,A,B,C)=\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{X}}\log\left(\frac{aA-bB+\sqrt{X}}{aA-bB-\sqrt{X}}\right)\,\,,

(286)

with
X=(aA-bB)^{2}-(A^{2}-B^{2}-C^{2})(a^{2}-b^{2})
.
Any higher values
j,l\in\mathbb{N}
can be reached by the differential identities
(see also Ref.
Bojak:2000eu
)

\displaystyle I^{(j+1,l)}_{D}=-\frac{1}{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial a}I^{(j,l)}_{D}\,,\qquad I^{(j,l+1)}_{D}=-\frac{1}{l}\frac{\partial}{\partial A}I^{(j,l+1)}_{D}\,,

(287)

the first requiring
a^{2}\neq b^{2}
the second
A^{2}\neq B^{2}+C^{2}
.

3.5.4
Recursive determination of Mirkes type III integrals

In Sec.
3.3
we developed the methods for the derivation of
relations between angular integrals. One should stress that
there have been attempts in this direction in the literature before.
For example, in Ref.
Mirkes:1992hu
Mirkes made an effort to
derive the recursive formula for a special kind of single-massive Neerven integrals.
We find that his formula is correct only in four dimensions
and here we discuss how to extend it to arbitrary dimension.
The subject of study is the single massive angular integral

\displaystyle I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C)=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{(-\cos\theta_{1})^{l}}{\left(A+B\cos\theta_{1}+C\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right)^{j}}\,,\qquad l\in\mathbb{N}\,.

(288)

In order to derive the recursive relation for the integral (
288
) we write it down in the
form of a double massive integral with two denominators and express it in terms,
which occurred before in our calculations
(see Sec.
3.2.6
):

\displaystyle I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\to\infty}\,I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)\,,\text{with}

\displaystyle I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\,\frac{\Big(\frac{1}{\beta}-\cos\theta_{1}\Big)^{l}}{\left(A+B\cos\theta_{1}+C\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right)^{j}}

(289)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{A^{j}\beta^{l}}\,\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\,\frac{(1-\beta\cos\theta_{1})^{l}}{\left(1+\frac{B}{A}\cos\theta_{1}+\frac{C}{A}\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right)^{j}}\,,

where the parameter
\beta
should go to infinity after deriving the recursive relations.
Here, the velocities of massive particles are chosen as

\displaystyle v_{1}=\Big(1,{\bf 0}_{D-3},-\frac{C}{A},-\frac{B}{A}\Big)\,,\qquad v_{2}=\Big(1,{\bf 0}_{D-2},\beta\Big)\,

(290)

The scalar products of the velocities read

\displaystyle v_{11}=v_{1}^{2}=1-\frac{B^{2}+C^{2}}{A^{2}}\,,\qquad v_{22}=v_{2}^{2}=1-\beta^{2}\,,\qquad v_{12}=v_{1}\cdot v_{2}=1+\beta\frac{B}{A}\,,

\displaystyle X=v_{12}^{2}-v_{11}v_{22}=\frac{(A\beta+B)^{2}+C^{2}(1-\beta^{2})}{A^{2}}\,.

(291)

Using notations from (
3.5.4
) the integral
I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)
can be
classified as a double massive integral with two denominators (with positive
j

and negative
-l
powers):

\displaystyle I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)=\frac{1}{A^{j}\beta^{l}}\,I_{j,-l}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22})

(292)

Next we obtain the recursion relation for

I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)
using the 4th type recursion relation (
224
)
derived for the integral
I_{j,l}^{(2)}(v_{12},v_{11},v_{22})

at
j>0
and
-l<0
. We have:

\displaystyle I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{B^{2}+C^{2}}\,\biggl[-B\,I^{j-1,l-1}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)

(293)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1-l}{j-l-1+2\varepsilon}\,\biggl(A+\frac{B}{\beta}\biggr)\,I^{j-1,l-2}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1-l}{j-l-1+2\varepsilon}\,\biggr(A^{2}-C^{2}+\frac{2AB}{\beta}+\frac{B^{2}+C^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\biggr)\,I^{j,l-2}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)\,

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{j-2l+2\varepsilon}{j-l-1+2\varepsilon}\,\biggl(AB-\frac{A^{4}}{\beta(B^{2}+C^{2})}\biggr)\,I^{j,l-1}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)\biggr]

Finally taking the limit
\beta\to\infty
we derive the recursion relation
for type III integrals for arbitrary dimension and valid
for
l\geq 1
and
j>0
:

\displaystyle I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{B^{2}+C^{2}}\,\biggl[-B\,I^{j-1,l-1}_{D}(A,B,C)

(294)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1-l}{j-l-1+2\varepsilon}\,\biggl(A\,I^{j-1,l-2}_{D}(A,B,C)\,-\,(A^{2}-C^{2})\,I^{j,l-2}_{D}(A,B,C)\biggr)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{j-2l+2\varepsilon}{j-l-1+2\varepsilon}\,AB\,I^{j,l-1}_{D}(A,B,C)\biggr]\,.

This is the main result of this Section. Note, that in specific cases

j=1
and
j=2
the recursions relation read

\displaystyle I^{1,l}_{D}(A,B,C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l\,(B^{2}+C^{2})}\,\biggl[\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\ \frac{l}{l-2\varepsilon}\,\dfrac{\Big(\frac{3}{2}\Big)_{\frac{l}{2}-1}}{\Big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\Big)_{\frac{l}{2}-1}}\ A\,I^{(0)}

(295)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\dfrac{1-(-1)^{l}}{2}\ \dfrac{\Big(\frac{3}{2}\Big)_{\frac{l-1}{2}}}{\Big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\Big)_{\frac{l-1}{2}}}\ B\,I^{(0)}+\frac{l\,(1-l)}{l-2\varepsilon}\,(A^{2}-C^{2})\,I^{1,l-2}_{D}(A,B,C)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{l\,(1-2l+2\varepsilon)}{l-2\varepsilon}\,AB\,I^{1,l-1}_{D}(A,B,C)\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle I^{2,l}_{D}(A,B,C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial}{\partial A}I^{1,l}_{D}(A,B,C)=-\frac{1}{l\,(B^{2}+C^{2})}\ \biggl[\dfrac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2}\frac{l}{l-2\varepsilon}\,\dfrac{\Big(\frac{3}{2}\Big)_{\frac{l}{2}-1}}{\Big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\Big)_{\frac{l}{2}-1}}\,I^{(0)}

(296)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{l\,(1-l)}{l-2\varepsilon}\,\biggl(2A\,I^{1,l-2}_{D}(A,B,C)\,-\,(A^{2}-C^{2})\,I^{2,l-2}_{D}(A,B,C)\biggr)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{l\,(1-2l+2\varepsilon)}{l-2\varepsilon}\,B\,\Big(I^{1,l-1}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)-A\,I^{2,l-1}_{D}(A,B,C;\beta)\Big)\biggr]\,.

To derive Eqs. (
295
) and (
296
), we used

\displaystyle I^{0,l}_{D}=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\,(-\cos\theta_{1})^{l}=\frac{1+(-1)^{l}}{2\,(1+l)}\ \frac{\Big(\frac{3}{2}\Big)_{\frac{l}{2}}}{\Big(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon\Big)_{\frac{l}{2}}}\ I^{(0)}\,.

(297)

In the limit
\varepsilon=0
(i.e.
D=4
) we exactly reproduce the
recursion relations (D.23) and (D.24) in Ref.
Mirkes:1992hu
.
The latter are valid only at
\varepsilon=0
and fail for
\varepsilon\neq 0
.

In case of
l=0
and
j\geq 2
we use the recursion relation (
212
),
which is in notations of type III integrals reads:

\displaystyle I^{j,0}_{D}(A,B,C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{A\,v_{11}\,(j-1)}\,\biggl[2\,(j-2+\varepsilon)\,I^{j-1,0}_{D}(A,B,C)

(298)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{A}\,(j-3+2\varepsilon)\,I^{j-2,0}_{D}(A,B,C)\biggr]\,,

where
v_{11}
is defined in Eq. (
3.5.4
). As before,
the recursion (
298
) starts with master integrals

I^{0,0}_{D}(A,B,C)=I^{(0)}
and

I^{1,0}_{D}(A,B,C)=I^{(1)}_{1}/A
.
In Appendix
G.6
using Eqs. (
295
)-(
296
)
we present analytical results for the integrals for
j=1,2
and
l=0,\ldots,4
.

By analogy with integrals (
299
)
one can pin down the similar integrals

\displaystyle J^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C)=\int\mathrm{d}\Omega_{k_{1}k_{2}}\frac{(-\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2})^{l}}{\left(A+B\cos\theta_{1}+C\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right)^{j}}\,.

(299)

It is clear that in this case we can do the same trick — introducing
the massive propagator
(1/\beta-\sin\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2})^{l}
with
\beta\to\infty

and choose the second velocity
v_{2}
and therefore the scalar product
v_{12}
as

v_{2}=\Big(1,{\bf 0}_{D-3},\beta,0\Big)
and
v_{12}=1+\beta\frac{C}{A}
.
One can see that the two similar types of integrals

I^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C)
and
J^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C)
are related upon interchange of two arguments

B\leftrightarrow C
as

\displaystyle J^{j,l}_{D}(A,B,C)\equiv I^{j,l}_{D}(A,C,B)\,.

(300)

4
Conclusions

In present paper we discussed and developed new ideas and methods for calculation of loop
and angular integrals in
D=4-2\varepsilon
dimensions in QCD, which serve as input for the study of
perturbative matrix elements relevant for different processes such as Drell-Yan,
SIDIS, etc. In particular, our paper contains two main topics — handling of loop and angular
integrals. In the first case we further develop the Pasarino-Veltman (PV)
technique
Passarino:1978jh
for dealing with tensorial
integrals. We proposed a covariant formalism for consideration of loop integrals with rich Lorentz
structure (so-called tensorial loop integrals) arising from decomposition of Feynman matrix elements.
The method is based on the use of the orthogonal basis [see Eq. (
5
)], which is formed
by specific linear combinations of external momenta, which is quite simple and convenient
for making analytical and numerical calculations. Note that our choice of the orthogonal basis
is by construction free of soft
singularities occurring in the limit of vanishing momenta.
Also it gives the straightforward results for the scalar
functions in the expansion of tensor loop integrals,
i.e. without solving a system of equations like in the original
PV method. All scalar functions are manifestly Lorentz covariant and depend on the set
of Mandelstam variables. In present paper we restricted ourselves to the consideration of
diagrams up to four external legs, but an extension of our method to more complicated diagrams
(e.g., with 5 or 6 legs) is straightforward and we commented on it.
All scalar functions are related via recurrence relations and finally presented in terms
of PV functions or in terms of hypergeometric functions.

In the second part of our paper we presented a detailed and systematic analysis of angular
integrals, which play an important role in the study of angular structure of QCD observables at
next-to-leading order in the expansion in strong coupling constant. In particular,
it is important to have complete and analytical set of these integrals for massive and massless particles
and perform their
\varepsilon
expansion up to desired order. Based on methods developed before in
Refs.
vanNeerven:1985xr
-
Mirkes:1992hu
we implement new ideas for consistent
and analytical treatment of angular integral with arbitrary number of propagators and
independent of their power and kinematical properties. We explicitly discuss our method for
splitting of the product of arbitrary number of propagators allowing to reduce it to the
product of one or two propagators in Appendix
D
.
Also we studied in detail the integro-differential properties
of angular integrals in order to derive partial differential and integral identities and
the recursion relations. For the first time in literature we derive a complete set of recursion relations,
which allow to deduce any angular integral with two
denominators in arbitrary power and with arbitrary on-shell properties.
We consider all particular cases:

1.

the powers of the propagators are of the same sign and positive;

2.

the powers of the propagators are of the same sign and negative;

3.

the powers of the propagators have opposite signs;

4.

the power of one denominator is simply zero.

Next important issue we considered is the systematic
\varepsilon
expansion of angular integrals up to desired order.
We demonstrated that this task can be solved for all types of integrals (i.e. for any
powers of denominators and for any kinematical situations). For some integrals, e.g.,
with negative powers for both propagators we derived a closed polynomial formula
for the angular integral. For other cases, based on the recursion relations it
is enough to perform the
\varepsilon
-expansion of the three
master integrals
I_{0,0}
,
I_{1,0}
, and
I_{1,1}
.
We developed a
Mathematica
package to reduce angular integrals
to master integrals and to perform the symbolic all-order
\varepsilon
expansion.
This allows for fast calculation of any
I_{j,l}
with arbitrary
j,l\in\mathbb{Z}
and
up to arbitrary order in
\varepsilon
expansion.
All technical details of our consideration of loop and angular integrals are placed in
the Appendices, including tabulated integrals with specific on-shell properties and
powers of the denominators.

Finally, we summarize our main results:

1.

We developed new formalism for reduction of tensor loop integrals in
D
dimensions
based on the PV technique
Passarino:1978jh
. Our formalism allows
to expand matrix elements through a set of Lorentz structures induced
by the basis of orthogonal linear combinations of external momenta.

2.

We discovered new properties of angular integrals:
partial fraction decomposition (reduction of a number of denominators in the integrand);
integro-differential identities leading to derivation of recursion relations between
angular integrals with arbitrary indices; hypergeometric representation for all two-denominators
angular integrals including double-massive case was derived.

3.

We formulated approach for all order
\varepsilon
-expansion of angular integrals,
which is based on expansion of the set of three parent integrals, while the expansion
of the others is performed with the use of recursion relations.
The recursion starts with the parent integrals.

Acknowledgements.

We thank Werner Vogelsang for stimulating and useful discussions.
This work was funded by BMBF “Verbundprojekt 05P2018 - Ausbau von ALICE
am LHC: Jets und partonische Struktur von Kernen”
(Förderkennzeichen: 05P18VTCA1),
by ANID PIA/APOYO AFB180002 (Chile)
and by FONDECYT (Chile) under Grant No. 1191103.
This study was supported by Deutsche Forschungsgemeinschaft (DFG) through
the Research Unit FOR 2926 “Next Generation pQCD for
Hadron Structure: Preparing for the EIC” (Project number 40824754).

Appendix A
Triangle integrals

Here, we present analytical results for the scalar functions occurring
in the expansion of the triangle vector and tensorial (rank-2)
loop integrals.

A.1
Scalar and vector triangle integrals

First, we discuss calculation of scalar triangle
integral
C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})
with two massive external legs.
Here, we follow derivation done in Ref.
Smirnov:2006ry
.
We start with the integral

\displaystyle C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(i\pi^{2})^{-1}\mu^{4-D}\,\int d^{D}k\,\frac{1}{k^{2}(k+l_{1})^{2}(k+l_{2})^{2}}\,,

(301)

where
s_{1}=l_{1}^{2}
,
s_{2}=l_{2}^{2}
,
s_{3}=(l_{1}-l_{2})^{2}=0
.
Note that for
s_{3}\neq 0
the
C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
is fully
symmetric under permutation of the arguments
s_{1}
,
s_{2}
, and
s_{3}
.
Therefore, result, which will be obtained for
s_{3}=0
and
s_{1},s_{2}\neq 0

is trivially extended to other two cases of one vanishing mass:

s_{1}=0
,
s_{2},s_{3}\neq 0
, and
s_{2}=0
,
s_{1},s_{3}\neq 0
.

After
\alpha
parametization and integration over loop
momentum, the integral
C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})
takes the form

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptC_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})=-(\pi\mu^{-2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,\Gamma\Big(3-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha_{1}\ldots\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha_{3}\,\frac{\delta\biggl(1-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\alpha_{i}\biggr)\,\biggl(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\alpha_{i}\biggr)^{3-D}}{\Big(-s_{1}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{3}-s_{2}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3}\Big)^{3-\frac{D}{2}}}\,.

(302)

Then we apply the change of variables (
\alpha
parameters)
using the trick from
Smirnov:2006ry
:

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}=\eta_{1}\xi\,,\qquad\alpha_{2}=\eta_{1}(1-\xi)\,,\qquad\alpha_{3}=\eta_{2}\,.

(303)

Subsequently we take into account that the Jacobian of such
the change of variables is
J=\eta_{1}
and
integrate over
\eta_{2}
using
\delta
function.

One gets:

\displaystyle C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})=-(\pi\mu^{-2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,\Gamma\Big(3-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\xi\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\eta\,\frac{\eta^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,(1-\eta)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}}{\Big(-s_{1}\xi-s_{2}(1-\xi)\Big)^{3-\frac{D}{2}}}\,.

(304)

Next we integrate over
\eta
using the integral representation
for the beta function

\displaystyle B(m,n)=\frac{\Gamma(m)\Gamma(n)}{\Gamma(m+n)}=\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\eta\,\eta^{m-1}\,(1-\eta)^{n-1}\,,

(305)

where
\Gamma(m)
is the gamma function.
Integration over
\xi
proceeds using simple
table integral

\displaystyle\int\limits_{0}^{1}\,\frac{d\xi}{(\xi+A)^{n+1}}=\frac{A^{-n}-(1+A)^{-n}}{n}\,.

(306)

After these tricks the scalar triangle integral takes the form

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274ptC_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})=\pi^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,\frac{2\Gamma\Big(2-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\Gamma^{2}\Big(\frac{D}{2}-1\Big)}{(4-D)\,\Gamma(D-4)}\,\frac{\Big(-\frac{s_{1}}{\mu^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,-\,\Big(-\frac{s_{2}}{\mu^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{D}{2}-2}}{s_{1}\,-\,s_{2}}\,.

(307)

Next using the definition of the scalar bubble function

B_{0}(s)
(
9
) we express
C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})
in
terms of
B_{0}(s)
functions as:

\displaystyle C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})=\frac{2\,(D-3)}{4-D}\ \frac{B_{0}(s_{1})-B_{0}(s_{2})}{s_{1}-s_{2}}\,,

(308)

which is manifestly symmetric under change of variables
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
.
For the
C_{0}
function depending on one argument we introduce the following
notation

\displaystyle C_{0}(s)=C_{0}(s,0)=C_{0}(0,s)\,.

(309)

Now we show results for the vector integrals:

\displaystyle C^{\mu;0ij}_{1}=(i\pi^{2})^{-1}\,\mu^{4-D}\,\int d^{D}k\,\frac{k^{\mu}}{\Delta_{0}\,\Delta_{i}\,\Delta_{j}}=P^{\mu}\,C_{1P}^{0ij}+R^{\mu}\,C_{1R}^{0ij}+T^{\mu}\,C_{1T}^{0ij}\,.

(310)

All scalar functions
C_{1P}^{0ij}
,
C_{1R}^{0ij}
, and

C_{1T}^{0ij}
are expressed in terms
of scalar function (
29
) manifestly symmetric
under
s_{1}\leftrightarrow s_{2}
permutation:

\displaystyle C_{1}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}}{\lambda(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})}\,\biggl[B_{0}(s_{1})-B_{0}(s_{3})-s_{2}\,C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\biggr]\,,

(311)

For specific limits it reduces to:

Limit I (
s_{3}=0
)

\displaystyle C_{1}(s_{1},s_{2},0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}}{(s_{1}-s_{2})^{2}}\,\biggl[B_{0}(s_{1})-B_{0}(0)-s_{2}\,C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})\biggr]

(312)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}}{(s_{1}-s_{2})^{2}}\,\biggl[\frac{4-D}{2(D-3)}\ s_{1}\,C_{0}(s_{1})-s_{2}\,C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})\biggr]\,.

Limit II (
s_{1}=0
)

\displaystyle C_{1}(0,s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s_{2}-s_{3}}\,\biggl[B_{0}(0)-B_{0}(s_{3})-s_{2}\,C_{0}(s_{2},s_{3})\biggr]

(313)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{s_{2}-s_{3}}\,\biggl[\frac{4-D}{2(D-3)}\ s_{3}\,C_{0}(s_{3})+s_{2}\,C_{0}(s_{2},s_{3})\biggr]\,.

Limit III (
s_{2}=0
)

\displaystyle C_{1}(s_{1},0,s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s_{1}-s_{3}}\,\biggl[B_{0}(s_{1})-B_{0}(s_{3})\biggr]

(314)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4-D}{2(D-3)}\ C_{0}(s_{1},s_{3})\,.

Limit IV (
s_{1}=s_{3}=0
)

\displaystyle C_{1}(0,s_{2},0)\equiv C_{1}(0,s_{2})=-C_{0}(s_{2})\,.

(315)

Limit V (
s_{2}=s_{3}=0
)

\displaystyle C_{1}(s_{1},0,0)\equiv C_{1}(s_{1},0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B_{0}(s_{1})-B_{0}(0)}{s_{1}}

(316)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4-D}{2(D-3)}\,C_{0}(s_{1})\,.

Limit VI (
s_{1}=s_{2}=0
)

\displaystyle C_{1}(0,0,s_{3})\equiv C_{1}(s_{3},0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B_{0}(s_{3})-B_{0}(0)}{s_{3}}

(317)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4-D}{2(D-3)}\,C_{0}(s_{3})\,.

One can see that Limits V and VI are specified by the same function

C_{1}(s,0)
.

In the following it is convenient to use the notations:

\displaystyle F(s_{1},s_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B_{0}(s_{1})-B_{0}(s_{2})}{s_{1}-s_{2}}=\frac{4-D}{2(D-3)}\ C_{0}(s_{1},s_{2})\,,

(318)

\displaystyle G(s_{1},s_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B_{0}(s_{1})+B_{0}(s_{2})}{s_{1}-s_{2}}\,,

(319)

\displaystyle H(s_{1},s_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G(s_{1},s_{2})+\frac{s_{1}+s_{2}}{s_{1}-s_{2}}\,F(s_{1},s_{2})\,.

(320)

Functions
F(s_{1},s_{2})
and
G(s_{1},s_{2})
are manifestly symmetric resp. antisymmetric
under exchange of variables
s_{1}\leftrightarrow s_{2}
. Function
F(s_{1},s_{2})
obeys the
useful identity:

\displaystyle F(s_{1},s_{2})=\frac{s_{1}\,F(s_{1},0)-s_{2}\,F(s_{2},0)}{s_{1}-s_{2}}\,.

(321)

Function
H(s_{1},s_{2})
has the following property::

\displaystyle H(s,0)=-H(0,s)=\frac{2B_{0}(s)}{s}\,.

(322)

Furthermore we use the following relations:

\displaystyle C_{0}(s,0)=C_{0}(0,s)=-C_{1}(0,s)=2F(s,0)\,\frac{D-3}{4-D}\,,

\displaystyle C_{1}(s,0)=F(s,0)\,.

(323)

Also we introduce the
functions
\alpha_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
,

\beta_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
,

\gamma_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
, and

\sigma_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

depending on three different
Mandelstam variables with
s_{1}\neq s_{2}\neq s_{3}
and
s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}=Q^{2}
:

\displaystyle\alpha_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(Q^{2}-s_{1})^{2}-(Q^{2}s_{2}-s_{1}s_{3})}{s(Q^{2}\pm s_{1})}\,,

(324)

\displaystyle\beta_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(Q^{2}-s_{1})^{2}+(Q^{2}s_{2}-s_{1}s_{3})}{s(Q^{2}\pm s_{1})}\,,

(325)

\displaystyle\gamma_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(Q^{2}-s_{1})^{2}+s_{1}(s_{2}-s_{3})}{s(Q^{2}\pm s_{1})}\,,

(326)

\displaystyle\sigma_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(Q^{2}-s_{1})^{4}+s_{1}(s_{2}-s_{3})(Q^{2}s_{3}-s_{1}s_{2})}{s^{2}(Q^{2}\pm s_{1})^{2}}\,.

(327)

It is important to stress that the numerator of
functions
\alpha_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
is
manifestly symmetric under exchange of variables

s_{1}\leftrightarrow s_{2}
and obeys the identities

\displaystyle(Q^{2}-s_{1})(Q^{2}-s_{2})-2s_{1}s_{2}

(328)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Q^{2}s_{3}-s_{1}s_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(Q^{2}-s_{1})^{2}-Q^{2}s_{2}+s_{1}s_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(Q^{2}-s_{2})^{2}-Q^{2}s_{1}+s_{2}s_{3}\,,

Another interesting identity reads:

\displaystyle\gamma_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\,\gamma_{\pm}(s_{1},s_{3},s_{2})=\frac{(s_{2}+s_{3})^{4}-s_{1}^{2}(s_{2}-s_{3})^{2}}{s^{2}(Q^{2}\pm s_{1})^{2}}\,.

(329)

In above formulas the set of variables
(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

is not fixed to a specific ordering of
(s,t,u)
variables,
i.e. all possible 6 sets of arguments
due to permutations of
s_{1}
,
s_{2}
, and
s_{3}
could occur.

Now we are in the position to list the scalar functions, which occur in
the expansion of vectors triangle integrals. In doing this we group the
scalar functions into pairs taking into account symmetric relations
between them.
Most of the relations between the scalar functions are derived
using the change of the external momenta:
p_{1}\to-p_{2}
,

p_{2}\to-p_{1}
,

k_{1}\to-k_{1}
,
q\to-q
leading to the
following change of Mandelstam variables and
the momenta from orthogonal basis:

s\to s\,,\quad u\to t\,,\quad t\to u\,,\quad P\to-P\,,\quad R\to R\,,\quad T\to-T
.
It concerns the pairs of the integrals

(C^{015}_{1J},C^{026}_{1J})
,

(C^{016}_{1J},C^{025}_{1J})
,

(C^{018}_{1J},C^{024}_{1J})
,

(C^{019}_{1J},C^{023}_{1J})
,

(C^{035}_{1J},C^{069}_{1J})
,

(C^{046}_{1J},C^{058}_{1J})
.
The relations between the integrals

C^{038}_{1J}
and
C^{069}_{1K}
are obtained
by inverse of the loop momentum
k\to-k
.
The relations between the integrals

C^{012}_{1J}
and
C^{017}_{1J}
are obtained
by change of the loop momentum
k\to-(k+p_{1})
,
while in the case of
C^{078}_{1J}
and
C^{079}_{1J}
integrals
we apply the following change of the loop momentum
k\to-(k+P)
.

We proceed using the following strategy:

1.

indicate the pairs of
loop integrals obeying specific symmetry relations;

2.

display the sets of corresponding scalar functions and relations between them;

3.

write down explicit results for the scalar functions.

Loop integrals
C^{\mu;012}
and
C^{\mu;017}

Sets of the scalar functions:

\displaystyle C_{1P}^{012}(s)\,,C_{1R}^{017}(s)\,,C_{1T}^{012}(s)\,,

\displaystyle C_{1P}^{017}(s)\,,C_{1R}^{017}(s)\,,C_{1T}^{017}(s)\,.

(330)

Relations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lccl}C_{1P}^{012}(s)&=&-&C_{1P}^{017}(s)-\frac{1}{2}C_{0}(s,0)\,,\\

A.2
Tensor triangle integrals

In this section we show results for the triangle tensor rank-2 integrals:

\displaystyle C^{\mu\nu;0ij}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(i\pi^{2})^{-1}\,\mu^{4-D}\,\int d^{D}k\,\frac{k^{\mu}}{\Delta_{0}\,\Delta_{i}\,\Delta_{j}}=g^{\mu\nu;3}_{\perp}\,C_{2g}^{0ij}+P^{\mu}P^{\nu}\,C_{2PP}^{0ij}+R^{\mu}R^{\nu}\,C_{2RR}^{0ij}

(357)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle T^{\mu}T^{\nu}\,C_{2TT}^{0ij}+\{PR\}^{\mu\nu}\,C_{2PR}^{0ij}+\{PT\}^{\mu\nu}\,C_{2PT}^{0ij}+\{RT\}^{\mu\nu}\,C_{2RT}^{0ij}\,,

where
\{AB\}^{\mu\nu}=A^{\mu}B^{\nu}+A^{\nu}B^{\mu}
.

All scalar functions are expressed in terms of three scalar functions
derived in Eq. (
2.2
):

\displaystyle C_{21}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2;g}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{D-2}\,\biggl[B_{0}(s_{3})+\lambda\,\frac{s_{1}\,C_{22}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})+s_{2}\,C_{22}(s_{2},s_{1},s_{3})}{(s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3})^{2}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\,C_{23}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})}{s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}}\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle C_{22}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2;l_{1}l_{1}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})=C_{2;l_{2}l_{2}}(s_{2},s_{1},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3})^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}\,\biggl[-(s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3})\,B_{0}(s_{1})+(s_{1}+3s_{2}-s_{3})\,B_{0}(s_{3})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2s_{2}\,\lambda}{s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}}\,C_{1}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle C_{23}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2;l_{1}l_{2}}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})

(358)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3})^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}\,\biggl[-s_{1}\,B_{0}(s_{1})-s_{2}\,B_{0}(s_{2})+(2s_{1}+2s_{2}-s_{3})\,B_{0}(s_{3})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{s_{1}+s_{2}-s_{3}}\,\Big(s_{1}C_{1}(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})+s_{2}C_{1}(s_{2},s_{1},s_{3})\Big)\biggr]\,.

Now we display triangle tensor functions.

Loop integrals
C^{\mu\nu;012}
and
C^{\mu\nu;017}

Sets of the scalar functions:

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{012}(s)\,,C_{2PP}^{012}(s)\,,C_{2RR}^{012}(s)\,,C_{2TT}^{012}(s)\,,C_{2PR}^{012}(s)\,,C_{2PT}^{012}(s)\,,C_{2RT}^{012}(s)\,,

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{017}(s)\,,C_{2PP}^{017}(s)\,,C_{2RR}^{017}(s)\,,C_{2TT}^{017}(s)\,,C_{2PR}^{017}(s)\,,C_{2PT}^{017}(s)\,,C_{2RT}^{017}(s)\,.

(359)

Relations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lccl}C_{2g}^{012}(s)&=&+&C_{2g}^{017}(s)\,,\\
C_{2PP}^{012}(s)&=&+&C_{2PP}^{017}(s)-\frac{1}{4}C_{0}(s,0)\,,\\
C_{2RR}^{012}(s)&=&+&C_{2RR}^{017}(s)-F(s,0)-\frac{1}{4}C_{0}(s,0)\,,\\
C_{2TT}^{012}(s)&=&+&C_{2TT}^{017}(s)\,,\\
C_{2PR}^{012}(s)&=&+&C_{2PR}^{017}(s)-\frac{1}{2}F(s,0)-\frac{1}{4}C_{0}(s,0)\,,\\
C_{2PT}^{012}(s)&=&+&C_{2PT}^{017}(s)\,,\\
C_{2RT}^{012}(s)&=&+&C_{2RT}^{017}(s)\,.\end{array}\right.

Explicit results:

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{012}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{21}(0,0,s)=\frac{F(s,0)+G(s,0)}{D-2}\,\frac{s}{4}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PP}^{012}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{22}(0,0,s)-C_{23}(0,0,s)}{2}=\frac{G(s,0)-F(s,0)}{8}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2RR}^{012}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{22}(0,0,s)+C_{23}(0,0,s)}{2}=-\frac{G(s,0)+3F(s,0)}{8}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2TT}^{012}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{s}{ut}\,C_{2g}^{012}(s)\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{012}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2PT}^{012}(s)\,=\,C_{2RT}^{012}(s)\,=\,0\,.

Explicit results:

\displaystyle C_{2PP}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{Q^{2}-2s}{2s}\,C_{23}(0,u,0)=-\frac{F(u,0)}{2}\,\biggl[\frac{1}{D-4}+\frac{Q^{4}}{4s^{2}}\biggr]-\frac{F(u,0)+G(u,0)}{D-2}\,\frac{t}{4s}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big[G(u,0)-F(u,0)\Big]\,\frac{Q^{2}-s}{8s}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2RR}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{t}{su}\,C_{21}(0,u,0)+\frac{C_{22}(0,u,0)}{4}+\frac{(Q^{2}-2t)^{2}}{4s^{2}}\,C_{22}(u,0,0)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{Q^{2}-2t}{2s}\,C_{23}(0,u,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2PP}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})+\frac{u}{4s}\,\,\biggl[2F(u,0)\,\frac{Q^{2}-u}{s}+F(u,0)-G(u,0)\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle C_{2TT}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{s}{tu}\,C_{21}(0,u,0)+C_{22}(u,0,0)=-\frac{F(u,0)+G(u,0)}{D-2}\,\frac{s}{4t}-\frac{F(u,0)}{2}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{t}{su}\,C_{21}(0,u,0)+\frac{C_{22}(0,u,0)}{4}-\frac{(Q^{2}-2s)(Q^{2}-2t)}{4s^{2}}\,C_{22}(u,0,0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{t-s}{2s}\,C_{23}(0,u,0)=C_{2PP}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{u}{8s}\,\,\biggl[2F(u,0)\,\frac{Q^{2}}{s}+F(u,0)-G(u,0)\biggr]

\displaystyle C_{2PT}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{21}(0,u,0)}{u}\,-\frac{Q^{2}-2s}{2s}\,C_{22}(u,0,0)-\frac{C_{23}(0,u,0)}{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F(u,0)+G(u,0)}{4(D-2)}+F(u,0)\,\frac{Q^{2}}{4s}+\frac{F(u,0)-G(u,0)}{8}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2RT}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{21}(0,u,0)}{u}\,+\frac{Q^{2}-2t}{2s}\,C_{22}(u,0,0)-\frac{C_{23}(0,u,0)}{2}

(364)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2PT}^{015}(s,u,Q^{2})-F(u,0)\,\frac{u}{2s}\,.

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{025}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PT}^{025}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2RT}^{025}(s,u,Q^{2})\,.

Relations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lccl}C_{2g}^{016}(s,t,Q^{2})&=&+&C_{2g}^{025}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,\\

\displaystyle\frac{(Q^{2}+s)^{2}}{(Q^{2}-s)^{2}}\,C_{22}(Q^{2},0,s)-\frac{2(Q^{2}+s)}{Q^{2}-s}\,C_{23}(0,Q^{2},s)\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2s}{u-t}\,\biggl[C_{2PR}^{038}(s,u,Q^{2})+\frac{1}{s}\,C_{2g}^{038}\biggr]\,.

Loop integrals
C^{\mu\nu;046}
and
C^{\mu\nu;058}

Sets of the scalar functions:
Sets of the scalar functions:

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PP}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,C_{2RR}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,C_{2TT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,C_{2RT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,

(380)

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{058}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PP}^{058}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2RR}^{058}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2TT}^{058}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{058}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PT}^{058}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2RT}^{058}(s,u,Q^{2})\,.

Relations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lccl}C_{2g}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})&=&+&C_{2g}^{058}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,\\
C_{2PP}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})&=&+&C_{2PP}^{058}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,\\
C_{2RR}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})&=&+&C_{2RR}^{058}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,\\
C_{2TT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})&=&+&C_{2TT}^{058}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,\\
C_{2PR}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})&=&-&C_{2PR}^{058}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,\\
C_{2PT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})&=&+&C_{2PT}^{058}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,\\
C_{2RT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})&=&-&C_{2RT}^{058}(s,t,Q^{2})\,.\end{array}\right.

Explicit results:

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{21}(Q^{2},t,0)=\frac{H(Q^{2},t)}{D-2}\,\frac{Q^{2}-t}{4}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PP}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{ut}{s(Q^{2}-t)^{2}}\,C_{21}(Q^{2},t,0)+\frac{\alpha^{2}_{+}(t,s,u)}{4}\,C_{22}(Q^{2},t,0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}_{+}(t,u,s)}{4}\,C_{22}(t,Q^{2},0)-\frac{\alpha_{+}(t,s,u)\,\gamma_{+}(t,u,s)}{2}\,C_{23}(Q^{2},t,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{F(Q^{2},t)}{2s^{2}}\,\biggl[\frac{((Q^{2}-t)^{2}+ut)^{2}}{(Q^{2}-t)^{2}(D-4)}+\frac{Q^{4}}{4}\biggr]-\frac{H(Q^{2},t)}{D-2}\,\frac{ut}{4s(Q^{2}-t)}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle F(Q^{2},t)\,\frac{Q^{2}(2Q^{2}+s)}{8s(Q^{2}-t)}+\Big[G(Q^{2},0)-F(Q^{2},0)\Big]\,\frac{Q^{2}((Q^{2}-t)^{2}+ut)}{8s(Q^{2}-t)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2RR}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{ut}{s(Q^{2}-t)^{2}}\,C_{21}(Q^{2},t,0)+\frac{\beta^{2}_{+}(t,s,u)}{4}\,C_{22}(Q^{2},t,0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma^{2}_{+}(t,s,u)}{4}\,C_{22}(t,Q^{2},0)-\frac{\beta_{+}(t,s,u)\,\gamma_{+}(t,s,u)}{2}\,C_{23}(Q^{2},t,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2PP}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})+2F(Q^{2},t)\,\frac{ut}{s^{2}}\,\biggl[\frac{1}{D-4}+\frac{Q^{2}-t}{4u}\biggr]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{Q^{2}}{4s}\,\Big[3F(Q^{2},0)-G(Q^{2},0)\Big]\,,

\displaystyle C_{2TT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{s}{ut}\,C_{21}(Q^{2},t,0)+\frac{(Q^{2}-t)^{2}}{(Q^{2}+t)^{2}}\,\Big[C_{22}(Q^{2},t,0)+C_{22}(t,Q^{2},0)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2C_{23}(Q^{2},t,0)\Big]=-F(Q^{2},t)\,\frac{D}{2(D-4)}-\frac{H(Q^{2},t)}{D-2}\,\frac{s(Q^{2}-t)}{4ut}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{ut}{s(Q^{2}-t)^{2}}\,C_{21}(Q^{2},t,0)-\frac{\alpha_{+}(t,s,u)\,\beta_{+}(t,s,u)}{4}\,C_{22}(Q^{2},t,0)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{+}(t,s,u)\,\gamma_{+}(t,u,s)}{2}\,C_{22}(t,Q^{2},0)+\frac{\sigma_{+}(t,u,s)}{2}\,C_{23}(Q^{2},t,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2PP}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})+\frac{F(Q^{2},t)}{s^{2}}\,\biggl[\frac{(Q^{2}-t)^{2}+ut}{(Q^{2}-t)^{2}(D-4)}+\frac{Q^{2}(Q^{2}-2t)}{4}\biggr]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle-\frac{H(Q^{2},t)}{D-2}\,\frac{ut}{4s(Q^{2}-t)}+\biggl[3F(Q^{2},0)-G(Q^{2},0)\biggr]\,\frac{Q^{2}}{8s}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q^{2}-t}\,C_{21}(Q^{2},t,0)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{Q^{2}-t}{Q^{2}+t}\,\biggl[\frac{\alpha_{+}(t,s,u)}{2}\,\Big(C_{22}(Q^{2},t,0)-C_{23}(Q^{2},t,0)\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{+}(t,u,s)}{2}\,\Big(C_{22}(t,Q^{2},0)-C_{23}(Q^{2},t,0)\Big)\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F(Q^{2},t)\,\frac{Q^{2}}{s}\,\biggl[\frac{(Q^{2}-t)^{2}+ut}{Q^{2}(Q^{2}-t)(D-4)}+\frac{1}{4}\biggr]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{H(Q^{2},t)}{4(D-2)}+\Big[3F(Q^{2},0)-G(Q^{2},0)\Big]\,\frac{Q^{2}}{8(Q^{2}-t)}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2RT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q^{2}-t}\,C_{21}(Q^{2},t,0)

(382)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{Q^{2}-t}{Q^{2}+t}\,\biggl[\frac{\beta_{+}(t,s,u)}{2}\,\Big(C_{22}(Q^{2},t,0)-C_{23}(Q^{2},t,0)\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{+}(t,s,u)}{2}\,\Big(C_{22}(t,Q^{2},0)-C_{23}(Q^{2},t,0)\Big)\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2PT}^{046}(s,t,Q^{2})-F(Q^{2},t)\,\frac{Q^{2}-t}{2s}\,\frac{D}{D-4}\,.

Loop integrals
C^{\mu\nu;078}
and
C^{\mu\nu;079}

Sets of the scalar functions:

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PP}^{068}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2RR}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2TT}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PT}^{078}(s,u,^{2})\,,C_{2RT}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,

(383)

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PP}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2RR}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2TT}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2PT}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,C_{2RT}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,.

Relations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lccl}C_{2K}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})&=&&C_{2K}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,\qquad K=g,RR,TT,RT\,,\\
C_{2PP}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})+C_{1P}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})&=&&C_{2PP}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})+C_{1P}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,\\
C_{2PR}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})+\frac{1}{2}C_{1R}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})&=&&C_{2PR}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})+\frac{1}{2}C_{1R}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,\\
C_{2PT}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})+\frac{1}{2}C_{1T}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})&=&&C_{2PT}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})+\frac{1}{2}C_{1T}^{079}(s,u,Q^{2})\,.\end{array}\right.

Explicit results:

\displaystyle C_{2g}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{21}(s,Q^{2},0)=\frac{H(Q^{2},s)}{D-2}\,\frac{(Q^{2}-s)}{4}\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PP}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(Q^{2}-s)^{4}}{4s^{2}(Q^{2}+s)^{2}}\,C_{22}(Q^{2},s,0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{F(Q^{2},s)}{2}\,\frac{D-3}{D-4}+\frac{Q^{2}}{8s}\biggl[G(Q^{2},0)-\frac{Q^{2}+4s}{s}F(Q^{2},0)\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle C_{2RR}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(u-t)^{2}}{4s^{2}}\,\biggl[-\frac{16sut}{(Q^{2}-s)^{2}(u-t)^{2}}\,C_{21}(s,Q^{2},0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{4s^{2}}{(Q^{2}+s)^{2}}\,C_{22}(s,Q^{2},0)+C_{22}(Q^{2},s,0)-\frac{4s}{Q^{2}+s}\,C_{23}(s,Q^{2},0)\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{F(Q^{2},s)}{D-4}\,\frac{(u-t)^{2}}{2(Q^{2}-s)^{2}}-\frac{H(Q^{2},s)}{D-2}\,\frac{ut}{s(Q^{2}-s)}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{Q^{2}(u-t)^{2}}{8s^{2}(Q^{2}-s)^{2}}\,\biggl[G(Q^{2},0)s+F(Q^{2},0)(Q^{2}-2s)\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle C_{2TT}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{s(u-t)^{2}}{ut(Q^{2}-s)^{2}}\,C_{21}(s,Q^{2},0)+\frac{4s^{2}}{(Q^{2}+s)^{2}}\,C_{22}(s,Q^{2},0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{22}(Q^{2},s,0)-\frac{4s}{Q^{2}+s}\,C_{23}(s,Q^{2},0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4s^{2}}{(u-t)^{2}}\,\biggl[C_{2RR}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})+\frac{4ut-(u-t)^{2}}{4stu}\,C_{2g}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2}\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PR}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(t-u)\,(Q^{2}-s)^{2}}{4s^{2}(Q^{2}+s)^{2}}\,\biggl[(Q^{2}+s)C_{22}(Q^{2},s,0)-2sC_{23}(s,Q^{2},0)\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{F(Q^{2},s)}{D-4}\,\frac{u-t}{2(Q^{2}-s)}+\frac{Q^{2}(Q^{2}+s)(u-t)}{8(Q^{2}-s)s^{2}}\,F(Q^{2},0)\,,

\displaystyle C_{2PT}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2s}{u-t}\,C_{2PR}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,

\displaystyle C_{2RT}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{u-t}{2s}\,\biggl[\frac{4s}{(Q^{2}-s)^{2}}\,C_{21}(s,Q^{2},0)+\frac{4s^{2}}{(Q^{2}+s)^{2}}\,C_{22}(s,Q^{2},0)

(385)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{22}(Q^{2},s,0)-\frac{4s}{Q^{2}+s}\,C_{23}(s,Q^{2},0)\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{u-t}{2s}\,\biggl[C_{2TT}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})+\frac{s}{ut}C_{2g}^{078}(s,u,Q^{2})\biggr]\,.

Appendix B
Box integrals

B.1
Scalar box integral

First, we discuss calculation of the scalar box integral
D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})
.
After
\alpha
parametrization and integration over loop
momentum the integral
D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})
takes
the form

\displaystyle D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\pi\mu^{-2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,\Gamma\Big(4-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha_{1}\ldots\int\limits_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha_{4}

(386)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\biggl(1-\sum\limits_{i=1}^{4}\alpha_{i}\biggr)\,\biggl(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{4}\alpha_{i}\biggr)^{4-D}}{\Big(-s\alpha_{3}(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{4})-u\alpha_{4}(\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{3})-t\alpha_{3}\alpha_{4}\Big)^{4-\frac{D}{2}}}\,.

Next we apply the change of variables (
\alpha
parameters)
using the trick from
Smirnov:2006ry
:

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}=\eta_{1}(1-\xi_{1})\,,\qquad\alpha_{2}=\eta_{2}(1-\xi_{2})\,,\qquad\alpha_{3}=\eta_{1}\xi_{1}\,,\qquad\alpha_{4}=\eta_{2}\xi_{2}\,.

(387)

Next we take into account that the Jacobian of such
the change of variables is
J=\eta_{1}\eta_{2}
,
integrate over
\eta_{2}
using
\delta
function,
and integrate over
\eta_{1}
using the integral representation
for beta function (
305
).

After these tricks the box integral takes the form

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptD_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})=(\pi\mu^{-2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,\frac{\Gamma\Big(4-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\Gamma^{2}\Big(\frac{D}{2}-2\Big)}{\Gamma(D-4)}\,\int\limits_{0}^{1}\int\limits_{0}^{1}\frac{d\xi_{1}d\xi_{2}}{\Big(-s\xi_{1}-u\xi_{2}-t\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Big)^{4-\frac{D}{2}}}\,.

(388)

Integration over
\xi_{2}
gives

\displaystyle D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\pi\mu^{-2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}}{u}\,\frac{\Gamma\Big(3-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\Gamma^{2}\Big(\frac{D}{2}-2\Big)}{\Gamma(D-4)}\,\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\xi_{1}\,\biggl(1+\frac{t}{u}\xi_{1}\biggr)^{-1}

(389)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\biggl[(-u)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\,\biggl(1+\frac{s+t}{u}\xi_{1}\biggr)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\,-\ (-s)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\,\xi_{1}^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\biggr]\,.

Next step is to identify the box integral as a combination of
Gauss hypergeometric functions
{}_{2}F_{1}
. We can proceed in two equivalent ways.

First, using
\alpha
parametrization for the first term in
Eq. (
389
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{AB^{n}}=n\,\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\alpha\,\frac{\alpha^{n-1}}{\Big[A+(B-A)\alpha\Big]^{n+1}}\,,

(390)

and integrating over
\xi_{1}
we write down the integral as
combination of three terms

\displaystyle D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\pi\mu^{-2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,\frac{\Gamma\Big(3-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\Gamma^{2}\Big(\frac{D}{2}-2\Big)}{\Gamma(D-4)}\,\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\alpha

(391)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\biggl[\frac{1}{t}\,\biggl(-\frac{(s+t)\,u}{t}\biggr)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\,\alpha^{2-\frac{D}{2}}\,\biggl(1-\alpha\,\frac{s}{s+t}\biggr)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\,\biggl(-\frac{(s+t)(u+t)}{t}\biggr)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\,\alpha^{2-\frac{D}{2}}\,\biggl(1-\alpha\,\frac{su}{(s+t)(u+t)}\biggr)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{(-s)^{\frac{D}{2}-2}}{su}\,\alpha^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\,\ \biggl(1+\alpha\,\frac{t}{u}\biggr)^{-1}\,\biggr]\,,

Next using integral representation for the
Gauss hypergeometric function

{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c,z)=\frac{\Gamma(c)}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c-b)}\,\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\alpha\,\alpha^{b-1}\,(1-\alpha)^{c-b-1}\,(1-z\alpha)^{-a}\,,

(392)

we get

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636ptD_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\pi\mu^{-2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,\frac{\Gamma\Big(2-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\Gamma^{2}\Big(\frac{D}{2}-2\Big)}{\Gamma(D-4)}\ \biggl[\frac{(-s)^{\frac{D}{2}-2}}{su}\ _{2}F_{1}\Big(1,\frac{D}{2}-2,\frac{D}{2}-1,-\frac{t}{u}\Big)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2-\frac{D}{2}}{3-\frac{D}{2}}\,\frac{1}{t}\,\biggl(-\frac{(s+t)u}{t}\biggr)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\ _{2}F_{1}\Big(3-\frac{D}{2},3-\frac{D}{2},4-\frac{D}{2},\frac{s}{s+t}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2-\frac{D}{2}}{3-\frac{D}{2}}\,\frac{1}{t}\,\biggl(-\frac{(s+t)(u+t)}{t}\biggr)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}\ _{2}F_{1}\Big(3-\frac{D}{2},3-\frac{D}{2},4-\frac{D}{2},\frac{su}{(s+t)(u+t)}\Big)\biggr]\,.

Further applying the identity

{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c,z)=(1-z)^{-a}\ _{2}F_{1}\Big(c-a,b,c,\frac{z}{z-1}\Big)\,,

(394)

for the second and third
hypergeometric function we get:

\displaystyle D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\pi\mu^{-2})^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,\frac{\Gamma\Big(2-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\Gamma^{2}\Big(\frac{D}{2}-2\Big)}{\Gamma(D-4)}\ \biggl[\frac{(-s)^{\frac{D}{2}-2}}{su}\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(1,\frac{D}{2}-2,\frac{D}{2}-1,-\frac{t}{u}\Big)

(395)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2-\frac{D}{2}}{3-\frac{D}{2}}\,\frac{(-u)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}}{t}\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(1,3-\frac{D}{2},4-\frac{D}{2},-\frac{s}{t}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2-\frac{D}{2}}{3-\frac{D}{2}}\,\frac{(-s-t-u)^{\frac{D}{2}-3}}{t}\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(1,3-\frac{D}{2},4-\frac{D}{2},-\frac{su}{(s+t)(u+t)}\Big)\biggr]\,.

Note this representation can also be obtained
from Eq. (
389
) identifying there the first term
with the Appell function using integral representation

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636ptF_{1}(a,b,c,d,m,n)=\frac{\Gamma(d)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(d-a)}\,\int\limits_{0}^{1}d\alpha\,\alpha^{a-1}\,(1-\alpha)^{d-a-1}\,(1-m\alpha)^{-b}\,(1-n\alpha)^{-c}\,,

(396)

and further using the identity transforming the
Appell function
into a combination of two Gauss functions
(see proof in the end of this section):

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b,c,2,m,n)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-b-c)m}\,\biggl[\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(1,c,b+c,1-\frac{n}{m}\Big)

(397)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(1-m)^{1-b}\,(1-n)^{-c}\ _{2}F_{1}\Big(1,c,b+c,\frac{m-n}{m(1-n)}\Big)\biggr]\,.

Now we are in the position to derive the final expression for the
box integral applying the analytic continuation formula for
the Gauss functions in the second and third terms
of Eq. (
395
):

{}_{2}F_{1}(a,b,c,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(c)\Gamma(b-a)}{\Gamma(b)\Gamma(c-a)}\,(-z)^{-a}\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(a,1-c+a,1-b+a,\frac{1}{z}\Big)

(398)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(c)\Gamma(a-b)}{\Gamma(a)\Gamma(c-b)}\,(-z)^{-b}\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(b,1-c+b,1-a+b,\frac{1}{z}\Big)\,.

Note that only the first term in the identity (
398
)
contributes to the expression for the scalar box integral,
while the second term vanishes since it generates
the equal and opposite in sign contributions in the
transformations of the second and third term in Eq. (
395
).

Final expression for the box
integral is (see also Ref.
Duplancic:2000sk
);

\displaystyle D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{su}\,\Biggl[\pi^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\frac{\Gamma\Big(2-\frac{D}{2}\Big)\,\Gamma^{2}\Big(\frac{D}{2}-2\Big)}{\Gamma(D-4)}\Biggr]\,

(399)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Biggl[\Big(-\frac{s}{\mu^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(1,\frac{D}{2}-2,\frac{D}{2}-1,-\frac{t}{u}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(-\frac{u}{\mu^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(1,\frac{D}{2}-2,\frac{D}{2}-1,-\frac{t}{s}\Big)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\Big(-\frac{Q^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\Big)^{\frac{D}{2}-2}\,_{2}F_{1}\Big(1,\frac{D}{2}-2,\frac{D}{2}-1,-\frac{Q^{2}t}{su}\Big)\Biggr]\,.

Note that in the on-shell limit
Q^{2}=s+t+u=0
we
reproduce the result for the scalar box given in Ref.
Smirnov:2006ry
.

The formula for the scalar box integral can be nicely written in terms
of the scalar bubble PV function

B_{0}(p^{2})
(
9
) as:

\displaystyle D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{su}\ \frac{D-3}{D-4}\ I_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,

\displaystyle I_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B_{0}(s)\ _{2}F_{1}\Big(1,\frac{D}{2}-2,\frac{D}{2}-1,-\frac{t}{u}\Big)+B_{0}(u)\ _{2}F_{1}\Big(1,\frac{D}{2}-2,\frac{D}{2}-1,-\frac{t}{s}\Big)

(400)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle B_{0}(Q^{2})\ _{2}F_{1}\Big(1,\frac{D}{2}-2,\frac{D}{2}-1,-\frac{Q^{2}t}{su}\Big)\,.

Now we make the
\varepsilon
expansion of the scalar box diagram.
The
\varepsilon
expansion of the Gauss function reads:

{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-z}-\sum\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{n}\,{\rm Li}_{n}(z)

(401)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{n}\,{\rm Li}_{n}(z)=1+\varepsilon\log(1-z)-\sum\limits_{n=2}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{n}\,{\rm Li}_{n}(z)\,.

The proof of Eq. (
401
)
is given below in this section.
Note that the
{\cal O}(\varepsilon)
term in the expansion of the
Gauss function does not contribute to the
\varepsilon
expansion
of scalar box due to compensation between the three
Gauss functions
in Eq. (
B.1
)

\displaystyle\log\Big(1+\frac{t}{s}\Big)+\log\Big(1+\frac{t}{u}\Big)-\log\Big(1+\frac{Q^{2}t}{su}\Big)

(402)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\Big(\frac{s+t}{s}\Big)+\log\Big(\frac{u+t}{u}\Big)-\log\Big(\frac{(s+t)(u+t)}{su}\Big)\equiv 0\,.

Therefore, the
\varepsilon^{2}
contribution of the Gauss functions
reads:

\displaystyle\beta(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(-\frac{Q^{2}t}{su}\biggr)-{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(-\frac{t}{s}\biggr)-{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(-\frac{t}{u}\biggr)

(403)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\log\biggl(\frac{-s}{\mu^{2}}\biggr)\log\Big(\frac{u+t}{u}\Big)-\log\biggl(\frac{-u}{\mu^{2}}\biggr)\log\Big(\frac{s+t}{s}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\log\biggl(\frac{-Q^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\biggr)\log\Big(\frac{(s+t)(u+t)}{su}\Big)\,.

The expression for
\beta(s,u,Q^{2})
can be simplified
using the reflection identity for the dilogarithm:

\displaystyle{\rm Li}_{2}(z)+{\rm Li}_{2}(1-z)=\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}-\log(z)\log(1-z)\,.

(404)

One gets
Duplancic:2000sk
:

\displaystyle\beta(s,u,Q^{2})={\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1+\frac{t}{s}\biggr)+{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1+\frac{t}{u}\biggr)-{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1+\frac{Q^{2}t}{su}\biggr)-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\,.

(405)

Next using well-known identity (Abel formula) relating 5
dilogarithms
Cachazo:2004zb
;
Ellis:2007qk

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\log^{2}\frac{s}{u}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1+\frac{t}{s}\biggr)+{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1+\frac{t}{u}\biggr)

(406)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1+\frac{Q^{2}t}{su}\biggr)+{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1-\frac{Q^{2}}{s}\biggr)+{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1-\frac{Q^{2}}{u}\biggr)=0\,,

one can represent the
\beta(s,u,Q^{2})

in terms of two dilogarithms
Ellis:2007qk
:

\displaystyle\beta(s,u,Q^{2})=-{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1-\frac{Q^{2}}{s}\biggr)-{\rm Li}_{2}\biggl(1-\frac{Q^{2}}{u}\biggr)-\frac{1}{2}\,\log^{2}\frac{s}{u}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\,.

(407)

Here for convenience we do not expand the
B_{0}
function and expand only the
Gauss hypergeometric functions.

Now we derive the
\varepsilon
expansion of the
Gauss hypergeometric function
{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-\epsilon,1-\epsilon,z)
stated in Eq.
401
.
We start with the Euler integral representation for this function:

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(1-\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(-\varepsilon)}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{-1-\varepsilon}(1-xt)^{-1}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{t^{-1-\varepsilon}}{1-xt}\,.

(408)

To get rid of the
\varepsilon
-pole in the above integral we perform a partial
integration.
3
3

3

Subtracting the pole like in Sec.
3.4

would work equally well.
Afterwards, we can safely expand the integrand in the

\varepsilon
series:

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{t^{-\varepsilon}}{1-xt}\right|_{t=0}^{1}-x\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{t^{-\varepsilon}}{(1-xt)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-x}-x\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\varepsilon)^{n}}{n!}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{\log^{n}t}{(1-xt)^{2}}\,.

(409)

Now we use the identity

\displaystyle x\,\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,\frac{\log^{n}t}{(1-xt)^{2}}=\mathrm{Li}_{n}(x)

(410)

which can be established by induction for all
n\geq 0
utilizing
the recursive definition of the polylogarithms via

\displaystyle\mathrm{Li}_{n+1}(x)=\int_{0}^{x}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\,\mathrm{Li}_{n}(t)\,.

(411)

Plugging (
410
) into
(
409
) gives

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-x}-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{n}\,\mathrm{Li}_{n}(x)\,.

(412)

With
\mathrm{Li}_{0}(x)=\frac{x}{1-x}
and
\mathrm{Li}_{1}(x)=-\log(1-x)

we arrive at the all order expansion

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-\varepsilon,1-\varepsilon,x)

\displaystyle=1+\varepsilon\log(1-x)-\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{n}\,\mathrm{Li}_{n}(x)\,.

(413)

The gamma functions in Eq. (
399
) generates
a
\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}
-pole, as

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(\varepsilon)\Gamma^{2}(-\varepsilon)}{\Gamma(-2\varepsilon)}=-\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma_{\text{E}}\varepsilon}\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)\right)\,.

(414)

To conclude this section, we prove the identity (
397
), which to our knowledge
occurs for the first time in literature. Our proof is based on
the use of the Mellin-Barnes integral technique.

We start with the integral representation

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b_{1},b_{2},2,x,y)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,(1-xt)^{-b_{1}}(1-yt)^{-b_{2}}\,.

(415)

We factorize both multipliers in the integrand applying the
Binomi-Mellin-Newton integral

\displaystyle(a+b)^{w}=\frac{1}{\Gamma(-w)}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\,a^{w-z}b^{z}\Gamma(-z)\Gamma(-w+z)\,.

(416)

It yields

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b_{1},b_{2},2,x,y)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}

\displaystyle\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}(-x)^{z_{1}}\,(-y)^{z_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma(-z_{1})\Gamma(b_{1}+z_{1})\Gamma(-z_{2})\Gamma(b_{2}+z_{2})\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{z_{1}+z_{2}}\,.

(417)

Evaluating the integral over
t
and substituting
z_{1}

by
z=z_{1}+z_{2}
lead to

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b_{1},b_{2},2,x,y)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}

\displaystyle\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\frac{(-x)^{z}}{z+1}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi i}\left(-\frac{y}{x}\right)^{z_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Gamma(-z+z_{2})\Gamma(b_{2}+z_{2})\Gamma(b_{1}+z-z_{2})\Gamma(-z_{2})\,.

(418)

The integral over the
z_{2}
variable now matches the Mellin-Barnes representation
of a hypergeometric function. It can be subsequently expressed through
its real integral representation to obtain

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b_{1},b_{2},2,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\frac{(-x)^{z}}{z+1}\frac{\Gamma(-z)\Gamma(b_{2})\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2}+z)}{\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\frac{1}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}
\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}
\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}}\times\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(-z,b_{2},b_{1}+b_{2},1-\frac{y}{x}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2})}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\frac{(-x)^{z}}{z+1}\Gamma(-z)\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2}+z)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2})}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{b_{2}-1}(1-t)^{b_{1}-1}\left(1-t\left(1-\frac{y}{x}\right)\right)^{z}\,.

(419)

Interchanging the order of integration and rearranging the terms leads to

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b_{1},b_{2},2,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{b_{2}-1}(1-t)^{b_{1}-1}

\displaystyle\times\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi i}\frac{\Gamma(z+1)\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2}+z)\Gamma(-z)}{\Gamma(z+2)}\,\Big(-(1-t)x-ty\Big)^{z}\,.

(420)

We recognize in the latter formula the
z
integral as another
Mellin-Barnes representation
of the Gauss function
{}_{2}F_{1}
, thus

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b_{1},b_{2},2,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2})}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{b_{2}-1}(1-t)^{b_{1}-1}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,b_{1}+b_{2},2,(1-t)x+ty\right)\,.

(421)

The Gauss function occurring in Eq. (
421
) can be further simplified
using symmetry under interchange of the first and second argument:

\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}(1,b,2,x)=\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}(b,1,2,x)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,(1-xt)^{-b}=\frac{1-\left(1-x\right)^{1-b}}{(1-b)x}\,.

(422)

Therefore

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b_{1},b_{2},2,x,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2})}{\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{b_{2}-1}(1-t)^{b_{1}-1}\frac{1-(1-(1-t)x-ty))^{1-b_{1}-b_{2}}}{(1-b_{1}-b_{2})[(1-t)x+ty]}

(423)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(b_{1}+b_{2})}{(1-b_{1}-b_{2})\Gamma(b_{1})\Gamma(b_{2})}\biggl[\frac{1}{x}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{b_{2}-1}(1-t)^{b_{1}-1}\left(1-\frac{x-y}{x}\,t\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{(1-x)^{1-b_{1}-b_{2}}}{x}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}t\,t^{b_{2}-1}(1-t)^{b_{1}-1}\,\left(1-\frac{x-y}{x}t\,\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{x-y}{1-x}\,t\right)^{1-b_{1}-b_{2}}\biggr]=\frac{1}{(1-b_{1}-b_{2})x}\biggl[\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,b_{2},b_{1}+b_{2},\frac{x-y}{x}\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(1-x)^{1-b_{1}-b_{2}}F_{1}\left(b_{2},1,b_{1}+b_{2}-1,b_{1}+b_{2},\frac{x-y}{x},\frac{y-x}{1-x}\right)\biggr]\,,

where in the last line we recognized again the integral representations
of Gauss and Appell hypergeometric functions.

The Appell function in (
423
) matches the reduction
formula (see Eq. (1) in section 5.10 in Ref.
Erdelyi:1981ea
);

\displaystyle F_{1}(a,b_{1},b_{2},b_{1}+b_{2},x,y)=(1-y)^{-a}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(a,b_{1},b_{1}+b_{2},\frac{x-y}{1-y}\right)\,.

(424)

Using (
424
) in (
423
)
yields our desired result

\displaystyle F_{1}(1,b_{1},b_{2},2,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-b_{1}-b_{2})x}\left[\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,b_{2},b_{1}+b_{2},\frac{x-y}{x}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-(1-x)^{1-b_{1}-b_{2}}\left(1-\frac{y-x}{1-x}\right)^{-b_{2}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(b_{2},1,b_{1}+b_{2},\frac{\frac{x-y}{x}-\frac{y-x}{1-x}}{1-\frac{y-x}{1-x}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-b_{1}-b_{2})x}\left[\phantom{}{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,b_{2},b_{1}+b_{2},\frac{x-y}{x}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-(1-x)^{1-b_{1}}\left(1-y\right)^{-b_{2}}\,\phantom{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,b_{2},b_{1}+b_{2},\frac{x-y}{x(1-y)}\right)\right]\,,

(425)

which we used in the main text (compare Eq.
397
).

B.2
Vector box integrals

Here we list the scalar functions occurring in the expansion
of the vector box integrals in the orthogonal basis
(P,R,T)
.
First, we specify fractioning identities needed to
reduce tensorial box diagrams:

Set of identities useful for simplifying the

D_{1}(s,u,Q^{2})
integral

\displaystyle 2kP

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}\,,

\displaystyle 2kR

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Delta_{0}+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}\,,

(426)

\displaystyle 2kT

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{5}+u+kP\,\frac{u+t}{s}+kR\,\frac{t-u}{s}\,.

Set of identities useful for simplifying the

D_{1}(s,t,Q^{2})
integral

\displaystyle 2kP

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}\,,

\displaystyle 2kR

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Delta_{0}+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}\,,

(427)

\displaystyle 2kT

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Delta_{2}+\Delta_{6}-t+kP\,\frac{u+t}{s}+kR\,\frac{t-u}{s}\,.

Set of the identities useful for the simplifying the

D_{1}(t,u,Q^{2})
integral

\displaystyle 2kP

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta_{0}-\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{5}-u\,,

\displaystyle 2kR

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Delta_{0}+\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{5}-u\,,

(428)

\displaystyle 2kT

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta_{0}-\Delta_{3}+kP\,\frac{u+t}{s}+kR\,\frac{t-u}{s}\,.

Here we use
p_{1}^{2}=p_{2}^{2}=k_{1}^{2}=0
.

The scalar functions occurring in the expansion of vector box
integrals read

\displaystyle D_{1P}^{0125}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\mu}}{P^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu;0125}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2s}\,\biggl[C_{0}(u,Q^{2})-C_{0}(u)\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle D_{1R}^{0125}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{R_{\mu}}{R^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu;0125}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2s}\,\biggl[-C_{0}(u,Q^{2})-C_{0}(u)+2C_{0}(s,Q^{2})\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle D_{1T}^{0125}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{\mu}}{T^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu;0125}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})

(429)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{s}{2ut}\,\biggl[C_{0}(s)-C_{0}(u,Q^{2})-u\,D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(u+t)\,D_{1P}^{0125}(s,u,Q^{2})-(u-t)\,D_{1R}^{0125}(s,u,Q^{2})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2ut}\,\biggl[(t-u)\,C_{0}(s,Q^{2})-(Q^{2}-u)\,C_{0}(u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle s\,C_{0}(s)+u\,C_{0}(u)-su\,D_{0}(s,u,Q^{2})\biggr]\,.

\displaystyle D_{1P}^{0126}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\mu}}{P^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu;0126}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})=\frac{1}{2s}\,\biggl[C_{0}(t)-C_{0}(t,Q^{2})\biggr]=-D_{1P}^{0125}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,

\displaystyle D_{1R}^{0126}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{R_{\mu}}{R^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu;0126}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})=\frac{1}{2s}\,\biggl[-C_{0}(t)-C_{0}(t,Q^{2})+2C_{0}(s,Q^{2})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+D_{1R}^{0125}(s,t,Q^{2})\,,

\displaystyle D_{1T}^{0126}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{\mu}}{T^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu}(s,t,Q^{2})

(430)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{s}{2ut}\,\biggl[-C_{0}(s)+C_{0}(t,Q^{2})+t\,D_{0}(s,t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(t+u)\,D_{1P}^{0125}(s,t,Q^{2})+(t-u)\,D_{1R}^{0125}(s,u,Q^{2})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2ut}\,\biggl[(u-t)\,C_{0}(s,Q^{2})-(Q^{2}-t)\,C_{0}(t,Q^{2})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle s\,C_{0}(s)+t\,C_{0}(t)-st\,D_{0}(s,t,Q^{2})\biggr]=-D_{1T}^{0125}(s,t,Q^{2})\,.

\displaystyle D_{1P}^{0235}(t,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{P_{\mu}}{P^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu;0235}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2s}\,\biggl[C_{0}(t,Q^{2})-C_{0}(u,Q^{2})-C_{0}(u)+C_{0}(t)-u\,D_{0}(t,u,Q^{2})\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle D_{1R}^{0235}(t,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{R_{\mu}}{R^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu;0235}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2s}\,\biggl[C_{0}(t,Q^{2})+C_{0}(u,Q^{2})-C_{0}(u)-C_{0}(t)+u\,D_{0}(t,u,Q^{2})\biggr]\,,

\displaystyle D_{1T}^{0235}(t,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T_{\mu}}{T^{2}}\,D_{1}^{\mu;0235}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})

(431)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{s}{2ut}\,\biggl[-C_{0}(t,Q^{2})+C_{0}(u,Q^{2})-(u+t)\,D_{1P}^{0235}(t,u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(u-t)\,D_{1R}^{0235}(t,u,Q^{2})\biggr]=\frac{1}{2ut}\,\biggl[(Q^{2}-u)\,C_{0}(u,Q^{2})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(Q^{2}-t)\,C_{0}(t,Q^{2})+u\,C_{0}(u)-t\,C_{0}(t)+ut\,D_{0}(t,u,Q^{2})\biggr]\,.

Relations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lccl}D_{1P/1T}^{0125}(s,u,Q^{2})&=&-&D_{1P/1T}^{0126}(s,u,Q^{2})\,,\\[2.84526pt]

Integral
D_{2}^{\mu\nu;0235}(p_{1},p_{2},k_{1})
